#2324. The turning point that inspired Celtic to big European victory against Besiktas

On Guy Fawkes night, Celtic finally provided some European fireworks. Martin O'Neill's side got their Europa League campaign up and running with an enthralling 3-2 win over Besiktas.

Junior Olaitain put the visitors ahead in the 54th minute, scoring from the rebound of his own penalty kick that was initially saved by Sam Johnstone.

Celtic Park was engulfed by an all too familiar feeling before O'Neill introduced Callum Osmand and Camilo Duran in the 60th minute.

Osmand scored the equaliser seconds after coming on, with Duran latching on to the youngster's through ball to round Alex Nubel and put Celtic ahead with 12 minutes remaining.

Orkun Kokcu's pile-driver levelled things up for less than 120 seconds as Duran struck again in the 88th minute to give Celtic a priceless victory.

Here are the key talking points from Parkhead...

Celtic's Callum Osmand scored against Besiktas. (Image: Andrew Milligan/PA)

O’Neill made five changes from Sunday’s defeat at Falkirk, most notably drafting Jordan Lotomba in for his first start at right-back.

The Switzerland international had played just 31 minutes in green-and-white hoops before Besiktas, making his inclusion all the more surprising.

Lotomba was tasked with keeping Besiktas' left side quiet though former Arsenal attacker Leandro Trossard and Ernest Poku kept him well occupied.

The 28-year-old sought to maraud forward when he could, but once tripped up on his own feet when attempting a series of step-overs in the opposition third.

The concession of a second-half penalty compounded Lotomba's tough evening. He was withdrawn in the 60th minute for Colby Donovan.

Elsewhere, Kasper Hogh, James Forrest, and Sebastian Tounekti made up a second string frontline.

Duran, Celtic’s top scorer this season, dropped to the bench along with Haissem Hassan. But it wasn’t a completely rotated XI.

Regulars Kieran Tierney, Cameron Carter-Vickers, Liam Scales, and captain Callum McGregor all began, with Benjamin Nygren and Mika Baur also in from the off.

That said, it was the first time O’Neill hadn’t gone full strength in Europe this season, perhaps indicating his feelings at Celtic’s current plight.

There are dull halves of football, and then there are dull halves of football. Besiktas enjoyed the majority of possession (61 per cent) in the opening 45 minutes without mustering many opportunities.

Celtic's sound structure limited the visitors' attacking play. Carter-Vickers defended ably but O'Neill's side struggled to get forward themselves.

By the break, Celtic had just one shot on goal, two touches in the opposition box, and 73 per cent pass accuracy, compared to around 88 per cent in their first three Europa League defeats.

Yet again, their inability to progress play through the lines was apparent. Celtic resorted to going long on a number of occasions. Hogh failed to make it stick and Tounekti struggled greatly.

Besiktas were quite content to pop the ball around, without taking any major risks. You still felt the Turks had a few more gears to move through.

Celtic shouldn't have needed a second-half wake-up call but just seconds after the interval, Trossard fired just shy of the left post.

Moments later, Johnstone's clearance ricocheted off the Belgian attacker and into Lotomba's right-back area. The Celtic defender slid needlessly in on Poku and referee Sascha Stegemann pointed to the spot without hesitation.

Olaitan stepped up and Johnstone made a heroic save that flew into the smoke-filled Glasgow sky, only for the initial taker to score on the rebound.

Just like Johnstone, Lotomba almost redeemed himself in the following seconds, driving forward from his defensive position before tumbling over the ball when attempting to shoot.

But Besiktas weren't ahead for long. O'Neill looked to his bench for fresh impetus on the hour mark and within just a minute of coming on, Osmand scored.

McGregor latched brilliantly on to a loose midfield pass and cut back for the substitute, who scored with practically his first touch.

Celtic Park had all of a sudden rediscovered its European voice.

For the goal, McGregor looked like his old self, driving forward from the engine room to kickstart a promising attack and Osmand provided the killer touch with his first European goal.

If O'Neill's plan was to wait until the game opened up in the second half and then call on the cavalry, it worked a treat.

Just 16 minutes after Osmand drew Celtic level with a fine strike of his own, he turned provider, playing a slide-rule pass in behind Besiktas' defence for Duran.

The Colombian got there ahead of Nubel and slotted beyond the goal line to turn the game on its head.

Duran didn't have to wait long to make his presence felt again, once more beating Nubel, this time from close range, after brilliantly controlling Tierney's mishit shot. It came seconds after Kokcu's leveller.

A true difference-maker, Duran is now up to 13 goals for the season. His impact could not have been greater here.

Well, who saw that coming? After four matchdays, Celtic are finally up and running in the Europa League. It really was now or never for O'Neill's charges, who have given their chances of progression to the knockout stages fresh hope with an unlikely victory.

Beyond their run of six defeats in ten leading into Thursday night, what made the win even more unexpected was the fact Besiktas are the best side they've played at home in the league phase so far.

Celtic struggled greatly against both Ferencvaros and Celta Vigo on their own turf, flailing whenever faced with adversity. They went down meekly in Lisbon too, losing 4-1 to Benfica last month.

No one should get carried away based on one result. Celtic still need somewhere in the region of six to eight points to finish in the top 24 and three out of their four remaining fixtures are away.

The next comes in Leiria against Portuguese second tier side Torreense after the international break. They could realistically gain three more points there before a trip to face Omonia Nicosia in December. Ties against Marseille and Union St-Gilloise lie ahead in January.

O'Neill will take great satisfaction from a European result that's long evaded his team. Time will define its influence on Celtic's wider Europa League picture.

#2323. Calciomercato no stop - Indiscrezioni, trattative e retroscena del 6 novembre

MILAN, GONCALO INACIO IL NOME CALDO PER LA DIFESA, NAPOLI, TRE PROFILI PER IL CENTROCAMPO. FIORENTINA, PER IL CENTROCAMPO IL PRIMO NOME È TORREIRA. PARMA, GILARDINO CHIEDE IL RINNOVO DI BERNABÈ. BOLOGNA, RAIMONDO IL CENTRAVANTI DEL FUTURO

Il mercato delle società di Serie A comincia a entrare nel vivo, con diversi club già al lavoro per programmare la sessione invernale e, in alcuni casi, anche le mosse per la prossima estate. In casa Parma tiene banco il futuro di Adrian Bernabé: il centrocampista, che la scorsa estate aveva una valutazione di circa 25 milioni di euro, potrebbe lasciare l'Emilia a parametro zero. Il feeling mai sbocciato con Cuesta e l'insoddisfazione per il ruolo ricoperto potrebbero spingerlo verso una nuova esperienza. Alberto Gilardino, invece, vorrebbe trattenerlo e punta a incontrare il suo agente prima della sosta per discutere il rinnovo. La Fiorentina cerca rinforzi soprattutto a centrocampo e in difesa: Ederson e Ferguson sarebbero profili ideali, ma difficilmente raggiungibili a gennaio. La pista più concreta rimane quella di Lucas Torreira, che vorrebbe tornare a Firenze e potrebbe rappresentare una soluzione per il reparto di Vanoli. Il Bologna guarda invece al futuro di Antonio Raimondo, attualmente protagonista con il Frosinone e autore di otto gol in dieci partite. La Juventus lo sta monitorando da settimane, ma i ciociari non intendono privarsene facilmente già a gennaio. Per la prossima stagione il Bologna dovrà decidere se puntare sul proprio attaccante oppure monetizzare la sua cessione. Sempre in casa Frosinone, attenzione a Fares Ghedjemis: dopo l'interesse del Monaco in estate, la valutazione è salita a 25 milioni di euro. Il club sarebbe disposto a privarsene soltanto davanti a un'offerta importante, preferibilmente da una società impegnata nelle competizioni europee.

Sul fronte delle big, il Napoli prepara un mercato invernale importante, soprattutto a centrocampo, dove potrebbero arrivare due innesti anche alla luce delle incertezze sul futuro di Lobotka e Anguissa. Nel mirino ci sono due talenti del Monaco, entrambi classe 2004: Mamadou Coulibaly e Lamine Camara. Tra le alternative figurano Gabriel Sara del Galatasaray, valutato circa 25 milioni, Bakola del Sassuolo, Bergvall del Tottenham e Aranda del Boca Juniors. Attenzione anche all'attacco, con Varela del Monza in cima alla lista qualora Lucca dovesse partire nuovamente in prestito. Il Milan si prepara ad accogliere Markus Krösche, nuovo Head of Football rossonero, ufficialmente operativo dal 1° gennaio 2027, ma intenzionato ad anticipare il proprio ingresso per programmare già il mercato invernale. La priorità resta un difensore, con Gonçalo Inácio dello Sporting Lisbona in cima alla lista, nonostante una richiesta economica elevata. Piacciono anche il giovane Luca Reggiani del Borussia Dortmund e Massiré Sylla dell'Union Saint-Gilloise. Previsto inoltre un intervento sull'esterno, in funzione anche dei recuperi di Saelemaekers e Chukwueze. L'Inter segue invece Chris Richards del Crystal Palace: il difensore statunitense piace particolarmente a Chivu e potrebbe arrivare già a gennaio, oppure a parametro zero nell'estate 2027, quando il suo contratto sarà in scadenza se non verrà rinnovato. Infine, l'Udinese guarda a più reparti in vista di gennaio: per la difesa torna d'attualità Mass Willemsen, classe 2003 dell'Heerenveen, già cercato in estate, mentre per il centrocampo è seguito Tchaouna, classe 2005 del Catanzaro e nazionale del Ciad.

IL PADRE DI NEYMAR NUOVO CEO DEL SANTOS. BAYERN, ORA IL RINNOVO DI OLISE. LE BRIS RISCHIA IL POSTO AL SUNDERLAND, VICENS SI APPRESTA A DIVENTARE TECNICO DEL LIONE Nuovo capitolo nel rapporto tra la famiglia Neymar e il Santos. Il padre della stella brasiliana è stato nominato ufficialmente amministratore delegato del club paulista, dopo l'accordo raggiunto con il presidente Marcelo Teixeira. Il Bayern Monaco non si ferma a Kane e ora pensa ad un'altra stella che sta brillando sempre di più e che necessita di un adeguamento di stipendio al rialzo: Michael Olise. Il suo contratto dura fino al 2029, e ci siederemo al tavolo per discutere le cose al momento opportuno". Il Sunderland ha subito un'altra pesante sconfitta casalinga, per 2-0 contro la Dinamo Zagabria nel match di Europa League giocato allo Stadium of Light. Un inizio di stagione tremendo per il Sunderland, attualmente ultimo in Premier League dopo 9 giornate con 5 punti (1 vittoria, 2 pareggi e 6 sconfitte). E in Inghilterra si parla di cambio di panchina in vista, con Roger Le Bris che rischia il posto nonostante i brillanti risultati degli scorsi anni. Il Lione accelera per il sostituto di Pauloa Fonseca, dopo essere sceso in campo nell'1-1 con la Real Sociedad con Jorge Maciel temporaneamente alla guida della squadra. Carlos Vicens, ex vice di Pep Guardiola, è più di una suggestione: fonti interne al club che avrebbero confermato che dovrebbe essere lui il nuovo tecnico.

#2321. Scott Parker can make early West Ham statement by entrusting 19-year-old with starting role after Cardiff City impact: Opinion

West Ham started the Scott Parker era in dramatic fashion, as they came from 3-0 down to draw 3-3 away at Cardiff City. WHUFC’s new boss did not hesitate to make changes at the break in the bid to inspire a turnaround either.

Parker made three substitutions at half time, with Joel Piroe replacing Taty Castellanos, Mohamadou Kante coming on for Soungoutou Magassa and youngster Ezra Mayers taking Morato’s place at left-back.

19-year-old Mayers is the reigning Academy Player of the Year and has been around the first-team for much of the season. This was his first Championship appearance though, and Mayers’ impact has given Parker food for thought ahead of the clash with Blackburn Rovers.

Morato has been solid at left-back for West Ham. He has offered a more defensive presence while the starting right-back – usually Kyle Walker-Peters – is given more freedom to progress and link up with star player Jarrod Bowen in attack.

The Brazilian struggled on Wednesday night though, conceding a penalty as Jack Moylan ran riot. When deciding to replace Morato at the break, Parker had two academy graduates to pick from: the more experienced Ollie Scarles, or Mayers.

He went with the latter, and was rewarded for his faith. Mayers offered a front-footed and aggressive approach in his defending as he looked to win the ball back and set the Hammers on their way. The youngster made one tackle and one block, winning one ground duel and even volleying a late chance past the far post (all stats via FotMob).

Watching Mayers, he showed the hunger and desire that WHUFC fans want to see from players – just like new boss Parker did when he was running the show in the Hammers’ midfield.

Parker’s appointment has pleased the majority of supporters. Speaking to fans, you may hear them say ‘He’s West Ham’ – the energy, aggression and passion he played with in the midfield made him a firm favourite and a real standout, winning their Player of the Year award for three consecutive seasons from 2009 to 2011.

WHUFC fans are eager to see Parker manage in a similar way by setting up his team to be intense and fast-paced. Mayers’ substitute appearance showed just this against Cardiff City, and these attributes could see him force a way into the plans.

Pardon the cliche, but Parker ‘gets it’ at West Ham – he understands what it means to play for the club and knows what the fans want from those donning claret and blue. In his brief Cardiff appearance and academy displays, Mayers has shown this too.

By putting faith in Mayers and handing him a first league start for the club, Parker would be making a popular move that could set the tone for his tenure.

#2320. Wouter Vrancken demands unity as Hearts seek to bounce back from Thun defeat

Wouter Vrancken insists Hearts will have to stick together following their self-inflicted 3-1 Conference League defeat by Thun in Switzerland.

The Scottish outfit, without 10 players through various injuries, fell behind in the 22nd minute when defender Jamie McCart knocked the ball into his own net.

Thun doubled their lead in the 33rd minute through striker Brighton Labeau after a poor attempted clearance from captain Stephen Kingsley – who went off later with an injury – and then Furkan Dursun grabbed a third in the 56th minute.

Jambos defender Stuart Findlay then gave some unrealised hope of a comeback in the 82nd minute when he headed in from close range.

After three Conference League fixtures Hearts have one point out of nine and have won just two of their last 10 fixtures in all competitions.

Vrancken told TNT Sports: “Like I said already to the guys, we have to stick together now.

“We know which situation we’re in with all the circumstances.

“And there’s only one thing that you have to do now and that is stick together and keep working hard.

“In general, it was not good enough in quality and can you blame them?

“Yeah, on one hand you can because you want to always expect the best and want the best.

“On the other hand, I’m still proud of what they did already in the run-up games.”

Kingsley was replaced in the second half by 19-year-old Matthew Gillies, called back from a loan spell at Stenhousemuir amid the club’s injury crisis, and Vrancken admitted there was an element of risk playing him on the artificial surface which the Jambos boss had criticised.

He said: “We talked it through before the game of course, a few days before the game.

“And yeah, it’s always a risk, especially when it’s another surface, and especially when it’s bad like that, but he was confident in playing and that’s where we are.”

Hearts’ next Conference League game is against Ligue 1 side in Monaco at Tynecastle on November 26 but Vrancken is more concerned about the home William Hill Premiership game against Falkirk on Sunday.

He said: “Monaco’s a really strong side, maybe the strongest side we will face in Europe.

“So we will see when we get there, because I think there are a lot of important games before that, especially on Sunday.”

#2318. How much Celtic earned in Europa League win over Besiktas

Celtic claimed their first win in the Europa League this season as they beat Besiktas 3-2 at Parkhead.

Martin O'Neill's side came from behind to take their first three points in the competition after three losses up to that point.

And in bagging their first win, Celtic also earned a bonus payment from UEFA for achieving a win in the league phase of the competition.

UEFA offer bonuses to clubs for wins or draws in the league phase of the competition.

By beating Besiktas, Celtic banked €450k or £375k in prize money from UEFA.

The fee comes on top of the €4.31m paid to every club involved in the league phase of the Europa League this season.

Qualification for the knockout round would mean a further €300k from the UEFA prize pot, with Celtic guaranteed a payment for their final position in the league phase, regardless of whether they advance.

The bottom club in the league phase will earn €75k, with that figure increased by a further €75k for every position higher a club finishes in the table.

− victory in the league phase €450k, draw in league phase €150k

− qualification for the knockout round play-offs: €300k per club

− qualification for the round of 16: €1.75m per club

− qualification for the quarter-finals: €2.5m per club

− qualification for the semi-finals: €4.2m per club

− qualification for the final: €7m per club

The UEL winners can expect to receive an additional €6m

#2317. Middlesbrough team news and predicted XI to face Watford: David Strelec latest amid Will Lankshear concerns

Middlesbrough remain top of the Championship table despite suffering a disappointing 2-0 defeat away at Wrexham on Wednesday night. Kim Hellberg’s side missed the opportunity to extend their advantage at the summit but remain firmly in the promotion picture.

Boro are currently one point above Wolves in second place and two clear of Birmingham City, Stoke City and Swansea City. They were without key striker Will Lankshear in North Wales due to injury and struggled to find the firepower needed to get a result at the Racecourse Ground.

Watford, meanwhile, have endured a difficult start to the Championship season under head coach Alessio Dionisi. The Hornets sit 20th in the table after 14 fixtures and are just four points clear of the relegation zone.

However, they secured a much-needed victory last time out, beating Lincoln City 1-0 at Vicarage Road. Summer signing Stephy Mavididi scored the decisive goal to give Watford a welcome boost.

Hellberg will assess David Strelec’s fitness ahead of Middlesbrough’s trip to Watford on Saturday, but has hinted the striker may not be ready to star, as reported by Teesside Live.

Strelec made a positive impact from the bench during Wednesday’s defeat to Wrexham, offering better hold-up play during an 18-minute cameo. However, having only recently returned from injury, Hellberg believes starting him could carry too much risk.

The Boro boss said: “David has just come back and trained with us for only one week. We will see now, but, in general, I didn’t think he was fit enough, and there was too big of a risk to start him in a game now because of just two days in between.”

Lankshear remains hopeful of being available against Watford, although his absence was felt again on Wednesday as Kyle Joseph struggled to lead the line. Boro’s top-scorer also missed out on an England call-up due to his injury concerns.

Strelec’s campaign has already been disrupted by injuries, including a recent setback on international duty. Summer signing Rokas Pukstas and defender Alfie Jones remain out.

#2316. Willem II vs PEC Zwolle - prediction, team news, lineups

By Adepoju Marvellous | 05 Nov 2026 04:34

Willem II and PEC Zwolle kick off round 12 of the 2026-27 Eredivisie season with a clash at the Koning Willem II Stadion on Friday night.

The hosts are still searching for their first league win of the campaign, while the visitors aim to extend their unbeaten league run to four matches.

Willem II begin life under Jurgen Streppel after the 57-year-old’s recent appointment as head coach, and they will hope their new manager can hit the ground running, especially given their sluggish start to the season.

De Superkruiken claimed their first win of 2026-27 with a 4-3 triumph over Helmond Sport in the KNVB Beker, but then suffered a humiliating 7-0 defeat to reigning champions PSV Eindhoven—taking their loss tally to nine in 11 Eredivisie outings.

Already 11 points adrift of safety, Willem II are paying the price for one of the longest winless starts to an Eredivisie season. As the campaign enters its second third, they require a dramatic turnaround in fortunes to avoid the drop.

Three points against Zwolle would be the perfect start to Streppel’s reign, but Friday’s hosts have struggled at home against their upcoming opponents, winning just one of the last five meetings (L3, D1).

With 26 of their 39 goals conceded coming after half-time, Willem II’s fate could well be determined by their second-half performance. The prospect of facing a team that has scored just a third of its 12 goals in the opening 45 minutes offers little encouragement.

Though not as poor as Willem II, Zwolle also made a poor start to their league campaign, securing just one win from their opening eight matches. However, they have since won two of their last three, either side of a 1-1 draw with Utrecht.

Sandwiched between those three unbeaten league matches was a 2-1 cup defeat to lower-league opposition - arguably the most disappointing result of the season for De Blauwvingers. Nonetheless, they travel to Tilburg with renewed confidence.

Zwolle remain in search of a new head coach following Henry van den Vegt’s departure. In the meantime, caretaker manager Dennis van der Ree is gradually making a case for himself, as his tenure has coincided with an upturn in results.

A victory over Willem II in their final match before the international break would undoubtedly strengthen calls from the Zwolle fanbase for Van der Ree to be appointed permanently. The 47-year-old will be eager to bolster his reputation after a difficult spell with Groningen.

No team in the Eredivisie has converted a lower percentage of their big chances than Friday’s visitors (20.8%), highlighting a key area for improvement if they are to sustain their upturn in form.

Jaden Slory served a one-match suspension during Willem II’s defeat to PSV last time out, but the 21-year-old is available for selection here.

This leaves Justin Hoogma and Amine Lackhar, currently sidelined with knee and groin injuries, respectively, as the hosts’ two guaranteed absences.

Eighteen-year-old Givaro Rahajaan has not featured in a matchday squad since early September and remains out of contention for PEC Zwolle.

Sherel Floranus will miss a fourth consecutive match for the visitors, who are also without Filip Krastev due to an Achilles injury.

Younes Namli found the net in the most recent meeting between these sides, and the Dane will be aiming to make an impact once again.

Delanghe; Ter Avest, Stam, Dekker, Tjoe a On; Twigt, Zarrouk; Slory, Van Aalst, Van Crooy; Haen

Schendelaar; Van de Haar, Viergever, Graves, Aertssen; Sommer, Thomas, Van der Wal; Mbayo, Van Hooijdonk, Namli

Back in the Eredivisie after 11 years, new Willem II boss Streppel is tasked with pulling off what would be one of the biggest escapes in league history.

Given Zwolle’s current form and Willem II’s ongoing defensive frailties, the visitors are narrowly tipped to come out on top.

#2313. St Mirren vs Rangers: TV channel, live stream and kick-off time

Rangers return to Premiership action on Saturday night with a trip to Paisley to face St Mirren.

Derek McInnes’ side head to The Marbill Coaches Stadium looking to maintain their momentum and pick up another important three points, following their Premier Sports Cup semi-final win over Aberdeen last week

The match has been selected for live television coverage in the UK, with supporters able to watch the entire contest.

#2312. Why West Brom’s £2m summer addition is becoming a smart signing under James Morrison

West Brom missed their chance to move into the top two as they drew 1-1 with Southampton on Tuesday night. As they prepare to host Millwall this weekend, James Morrison‘s WBA are serious about fighting for promotion this season, winning three of their last five including an impressive 3-1 win over West Ham at the London Stadium.

The start that they have had comes off the back of a successful summer window. Under Morrison, WBA made a real effort to revamp the squad with more youth and several new additions were made with that in mind.

Jimmy-Jay Morgan has taken a lot of the plaudits after all his goalscoring exploits. However, another one of their additions is starting to break into the side, Rabby Nzingoula.

The Baggies spent a reported £2m on the 20-year-old after a decent second half of the season on loan at Nurnberg. He has been a hugely important player over the past five games where West Brom have gone unbeaten.

The 20-year-old arrived from Strasbourg with plenty of potential but relatively little experience of English football. However, three months into his Hawthorns career, he is adapting to the Championship quicker than many would have expected.

Nzingoula has gradually moved from an impact substitute to a regular part of James Morrison’s midfield, making 14 appearances and accumulating 710 minutes. Seven of those outings have been starts, while his average rating of 6.73 suggests he is providing a dependable contribution despite his young age.

There is also more to his game than simply breaking up opposition attacks. Nzingoula has recorded 41 line-breaking passes and created nine chances in his Championship minutes so far. His 1.14 chances created per 90 puts him in the 75th percentile, while his shot attempts rank in the 80th percentile.

These figures suggest West Brom have a midfielder capable of contributing further up the pitch rather than simply protecting the defence.

At 20 years old, Nzingoula has a good level of experience at decent levels. The WBA man has played 37 times in Ligue 1 as well as featuirng in Europe for Strasbourg.

His versatility is another positive. Comfortable operating as either a six or an eight, Nzingoula gives Morrison different options in central and wider midfield areas. His recent performances, including a 7.3 rating against Southampton on November 3, suggest he is beginning to find his feet.

There are still areas for improvement. He has yet to register a goal or assist, his aerial numbers are relatively modest and three yellow cards highlight the occasional risks associated with his aggressive approach.

Nevertheless, West Brom should be encouraged. Nzingoula is only 20, has already demonstrated Championship-level defensive qualities and is showing signs of becoming a more complete midfielder. For a reported £2m, the Baggies could have secured a very smart long-term investment.

#2310. Cagliari vs. Frosinone - prediction, team news, lineups

By Seye Omidiora | 05 Nov 2026 05:57

Winless at Cagliari since 2013, Frosinone head into Saturday's gameweek-11 fixture at Unipol Domus as the likely favourites.

The promoted side have thrived since returning to the top flight, sitting sixth in the Serie A table ahead of another visit to a ground where they have not won in over a decade.

Cagliari's start to the 2026-27 campaign has been mixed, with the Sardinians experiencing contrasting fortunes across the opening rounds.

Fabio Pisacane's men claimed four victories from their opening five matches, suffering an understandable home defeat to Inter Milan, before drawing 1-1 with Juventus in gameweek six.

That point appeared encouraging in isolation, but the Isolani have failed to collect any more since, suffering four consecutive defeats to Monza, Bologna, Roma and Lazio while notably surrendering leads against both capital clubs.

Those results leave Pisacane's men 12th in the Serie A table, although they could climb as high as eighth if the teams above them, Napoli, Bologna, Torino and Genoa, all drop points.

However, that outcome depends on the Sardinians ending their league losing streak against an in-form, high-flying visiting side.

Frosinone's return to the top flight after two years brought the customary uncertainty, but the Canaries have flourished in the big time.

They offered signs of what was to come during their opening-day defeat to Juventus at Stadio Benito Stirpe, where the Old Lady claimed a 1-0 victory.

Only Napoli have beaten Massimiliano Alvini's side since, with the Canarini securing six victories from their next nine matches to sit sixth in the Serie A table ahead of matchday 11.

Their only away league defeat remains last month's 3-0 hammering at the Maradona in Naples, while the Ciociari have claimed a 3-0 victory over Fiorentina and earned satisfying draws at Genoa and Atalanta, the latter finishing 4-4 in Bergamo.

If there were grounds for criticism of Alvini's troops, it would be their failure to capitalise on Genoa and Atalanta's underwhelming form when facing those clubs, with Daniele De Rossi's men sitting 15th in the table and the Bergamo outfit two places below.

Nonetheless, they enter Saturday's encounter seeking their first away top-flight victory since August, a result that would also end their 13-year wait for success at Cagliari.

Adam Obert will miss this weekend's clash after receiving a late red card for two bookable offences against Lazio last time out.

Alessandro Deiola and Roberto Gagliardini are doubtful due to head and ankle injuries respectively; however, Alieu Fadera (thigh), Mattia Felici (knee), Riyad Idrissi (knee) and M'Bala Nzola (thigh) are unavailable.

With two-goal forward Nzola absent, Daniel Maldini is the only available player to have scored more than once for the Sardinians this term and will aim to add to his five-goal tally against the visitors.

Antonio Raimondo was withdrawn after 30 minutes of Frosinone's eventual 2-0 victory over Torino, and the Capocannoniere contender is unavailable for the away side.

Raimondo's absence represents a significant blow, given that his eight goals account for a third of the Canaries' league tally of 24; Daniel Birligea should start, having recently scored twice in the 5-1 thrashing of Lecce on October 29.

Florian Grillitsch is also likely to miss out for the Canaries, having not played since September due to a thigh injury.

Caprile; Ze Pedro, Mina, Rodriguez, Sugawara; Adopo, Romano; Koffer, Maldini, Fazzini; Mendy

Palmisani; Oyono, Monterisi, Calvani, Bracaglia; Masini, Calo; Ghedjemis, Schmid, Kvernadze; Birligea

Raimondo's absence removes a significant source of goals for Frosinone, but Birligea's recent brace against Lecce suggests that the visitors retain enough firepower to trouble their struggling hosts.

Cagliari have repeatedly failed to protect promising positions during their four-match losing run, and the Canaries have our backing to end their long wait for victory in Sardinia.

#2308. 2025 summer signing is slowly becoming a key midfielder for Millwall under Alex Neil: Opinion

Millwall have been a very dangerous side since Alex Neil was appointed boss. After narrowly missing out on promotion following a Championship play-off defeat to Hull City last term, the club are looking to bounce back once again.

Neil’s side have built a reputation for identifying players capable of making an impact in the Championship without spending excessively. This summer has seen Kyrell Lisbie come in and impress, while Lyndon Dykes has been an excellent free transfer.

While immediate impacts are always looked for with transfers, patience is also required sometimes. Derek Mazou-Sacko is becoming another example of that, and is showing that Millwall’s long-term recruitment working well despite a mediocre start to the campaign.

The French midfielder arrived from Rodez in August 2025 for a reported fee of around €500,000. There was little fanfare surrounding his arrival, but he has gradually established himself as an important part of Alex Neil’s side.

At just 22, he is already showing the qualities needed to become a reliable Championship performer. Mazou-Sacko is not a midfielder who will regularly grab headlines with goals, assists or eye-catching creative numbers.

His value lies in the less glamorous aspects of the game, and his latest statistics underline why Neil has increasingly trusted him. His aerial ability is another useful asset, standing at 6ft 1in, That physical presence helps Millwall compete for second balls and provides additional protection in front of their defence.

There is little suggestion that he should be expected to offer much more creatively. His five chances created place him in the 11th percentile, while his 440 touches rank in the 16th. However, that should not necessarily be viewed as a weakness.

He has completed 267 passes at an accuracy of 83.4%, including 46 line-breaking passes, showing he can retain possession and move the ball forward without taking unnecessary risks.

His development has been gradual. Having progressed through Troyes, spent time on loan at Nancy and subsequently made around 34 appearances for Rodez in Ligue 2, the move to English football represented an important opportunity.

He contributed to Millwall’s play-off push last season, making 23 league appearances and registering one goal and one assist. Now approaching 35 appearances for the club, he looks increasingly comfortable at this level.

Millwall will need more than one dependable defensive midfielder if they are to achieve their ambitions, but Mazou-Sacko is proving to be an excellent piece of business.

He might never become the most exciting player in Neil’s squad, but he makes the team more difficult to play through. Over a 46-game Championship season, that reliability can be just as valuable as an attacking match-winner.

#2307. Chelsea news: three positives and two disappointments... verdict on five players sent to Strasbourg

By Axel Clody | 05 Nov 2026 07:02

Very active in the last summer transfer window, Strasbourg signed five players from Chelsea. A few weeks after their arrival in Alsace, what is the verdict on their first steps with the club?

Revived by a home thrashing of Auxerre (5-1) after four consecutive defeats, Strasbourg have had a stop-start opening to the season.

Probably not helped by too big a squad overhaul in the last summer window, with close to £85m (€100m) spent and 17 signings registered, the Alsace club are suffering the full effect of a transfer mistake recently highlighted by Ismael Doukoure: a sizeable quota of foreign players and not a single signing with any Ligue 1 experience.

Among the summer arrivals, Hugo Oliveira’s side signed five players from Chelsea, the club with which Strasbourg share the same owner, BlueCo, in a collaboration designed to make transactions between the two sides easier.

They are Brazilian forward Deivid Washington, who cost £13.6m (€16m), English attacking midfielder Omari Kellyman, who cost £5.1m (€6m), Danish goalkeeper Filip Jorgensen on loan, Portuguese defensive midfielder Dario Essugo on loan and Swedish right-back Genesis Antwi on loan. Five players with radically different playing time and performances since the start of the 2026-27 season.

A few months after their arrival in France, what verdict can be drawn? Three positives and two disappointments stand out.

On the positive side, Dario Essugo has made four starts in seven appearances, with two goals and one assist. In terms of presence on the pitch, Filip Jorgensen outstrips the competition with nine starts in nine matches and a status strengthened by wearing the captain’s armband in three games. Although he has not yet kept a clean sheet, he has made 32 saves.

Genesis Antwi, for his part, has started eight times, with Hugo Oliveira only leaving him on the bench against Auxerre. The 19-year-old has just been rewarded for his good performances with a first call-up to Graham Potter’s Sweden squad for the upcoming international break and the Nations League matches against Bosnia-Herzegovina and Poland.

For the two other Chelsea recruits, however, the verdict is not very impressive. Deivid Washington, Strasbourg’s second most expensive signing of summer 2026, has so far been a flop: the Brazilian has still not played a single minute.

Finally, Omari Kellyman has only one appearance, for just six minutes, in a 3-0 defeat to Rennes on 25 October. In other words, the pile-up of players in Strasbourg’s squad has not stopped some from standing out while others remain on the sidelines. It is one way of weighing up the pros and cons of the club’s huge summer 2026 recruitment drive.

#2305. Barcelona: Raphinha to extend contract until 2030, three factors behind new deal

By Axel Clody | 05 Nov 2026 07:10

There could not be a better time for Raphinha to have his contract renewed by Barcelona. On Thursday, Barcelona will extend the deal of the Brazilian, who is the team’s current captain, until mid-2030, according to Trivela.

As well as being handed the captain’s armband this season, the Brazil international forward is arguably the best player in the world since the competitions began in August. Playing as a centre-forward, he has become a goal machine: 19 in only 13 matches in 2026-27.

It is a revolution the forward has experienced at the Catalan club since 2024. Signed in 2022, he was criticised and even questioned his future at the club, wavering over proposals from Saudi football.

The turning point came with the arrival of Hansi Flick, who convinced him to stay and turned him into one of the best players in the world, first on the left wing. In 2024-25, the German manager’s first year, he set career records for goals (34) and assists (22). After a poorer last year because of injuries, he has returned in full force this season.

His good form was one of the reasons for the renewal, as Trivela learned from an anonymous source who had contact with the negotiations. But it is not the only factor.

Raphinha and his staff understood that extending the contract was the best path given the moment he is experiencing and, in particular, because he is treated by the manager and the club as an important player. This is evident in every word Flick says about the Brazilian, always showering him with praise.

‘He is playing at a spectacular level. His strengths and characteristics fit our style perfectly. He always finds spaces to attack, and we also have players who can provide the final pass. He is very focused, he is our captain and he is showing his quality, which is important for us,’ Flick said after the 6-1 win over Alaves last weekend.

The captaincy mentioned by the manager was his own choice to reward the leadership of the number 11. Raphinha has always been a reference for younger players, which built trust in the dressing room, according to Trivela.

Having a foreigner as captain of one of the biggest clubs in the world is a weighty matter, but it came naturally. The Brazilian has won over the fans and created an identification that also made a difference in the renewal. For that reason there were no obstacles and the negotiation went smoothly for both sides.

When the new contract ends, Raphinha will be 33.

He is already the fourth Brazilian with the most goals for the club, with 94, level with Ronaldinho and behind only Neymar (105) and Rivaldo (130). In the overall ranking he is already in the top 25. In the Champions League, he has just equalled Robert Lewandowski, both with 23 goals, and is surpassed only by Luis Suarez and Lionel Messi.

He is a player who is increasingly becoming a Barcelona idol and, if he maintains his individual level and adds collective trophies, could finish the season as the best in the world.

#2304. Who says Martin O'Neill is out of touch? Besiktas win was a tactical masterclass

The AGM that Celtic will hold at Parkhead on Tuesday, December 1, promises to be as explosive as any of the fireworks displays that were staged in the East End of Glasgow last night.

The Scottish champions’ performance in their home Europa League league phase match against Besiktas last night was, after a few damp squib displays in recent weeks, pretty incendiary too.

As rockets lit up the night air around the stadium and the sound of pyrotechnics being set off rang out, Martin O’Neill’s men finally found their sparkle.

Beating opponents who were sitting in the automatic qualification spots in the table alongside the likes of Benfica, Bayer Leverkusen and Juventus and avoiding an unwanted record was always going to be a big ask.

But O’Neill, the septuagenarian whose suitability for his high-pressure, high-profile role has been widely questioned in the wake of losses to Rangers, Ferencvaros, Celta Vigo and Falkirk, showed the flame still burns brightly.

The Northern Irishman, who selected Sebastien Tounekti, Kasper Hogh and James Forrest in his front three, vowed that his men would go for it when he spoke to BBC Radio Scotland ahead of the game.

However, the flat first-half display looked a little like a damage-limitation exercise, an attempt by a manager who knew his side was lacking in confidence after winning just three of their previous 10 outings to avoid a heavy home reverse and further opprobrium from the stands.

That was understandable to an extent. The visitors, who had beaten Hoffenheim and Marseille and drawn with Crystal Palace in their opening three fixtures, had Leandro Trossard, the Belgium international, pulling the strings for them in the middle of the park.

Still, an arena which is normally a cauldron of noise on big European nights and which is renowned across the world for its febrile atmosphere was eerily silent at times as the hosts sat back, concentrated on containing their rivals and offered next to nothing going forward.

Celtic left back Kieran Tierney at Parkhead last night (Image: Andrew Milligan)

Oh Hyeon-gyu, the South Korean international who spent a season-and-a-half at Celtic between 2022 and 2024 and won five major honours in that time, has clearly endeared himself to Besiktas supporters since moving to Istanbul in a €14m transfer in February.

The 25-year-old was given a huge ovation by the thousands of travelling supporters who had been shoehorned into the corner between the South Stand and the Lisbon Lions Stand when he emerged from the tunnel before kick-off.

He stood on the turf he once graced on a weekly basis in his bare feet and conducted a rendition of the “Oh Oh Song” which Black and Whites fans chant whenever he nets a goal. Eat your heart out Graham Roberts.

Oh is one of those players, and there have been quite a few of them over the years, who goes from strength to strength after departing Celtic. The 6ft 2in striker would have improved the hosts’ forward line in the first half last night.

Benjamin Nygren had the perfect chance to put the home team ahead and set proceedings alight in the seventh minute when he received the ball from Sebastien Tounekti in space in the opposition area. The Swede had time to control the pass and place his shot. He fired over the crossbar.

It was the only attempt on goal in the opening 45 minutes.

Vaclav Cerny, the former Rangers winger who returned to Parkhead for the first time since he sprayed Celtic fans with a water bottle after a 3-2 derby victory in March last year, was booed by the home support every time he got on the ball.

There was a huge roar in the first half when Liam Scales slid in and scythed the Czech down. There was precious little else for the green-and-white-clad hordes who had turned up – and thousands of green seats lay empty – to shout about.

Preferring the experience of Jordan Lotomba to the raw youthful exuberance of Colby Donovan was, regardless of the Swiss full-back’s lack of recent game time, a smart move.

The defender’s rustiness perhaps showed when he conceded a penalty with a rash foul on Ernest Poku. Junior Olaitan netted after Sam Johnstone had saved his initial effort.

Celtic manager Martin O'Neill at Parkhead last night (Image: Andrew Milligan)

It was a cruel, even unfair, setback. Celtic had not looked like conceding up until that point. But O’Neill threw on Donovan, Callum Osmand and Camilo Duran for Lotomba, Tounekti and Forrest respectively. His inspired substitutions changed the game completely.

The 74-year-old has been portrayed as an old-school manager who is out of touch with the modern game in certain quarters in recent weeks as his charges have toiled and suffered painful losses. He silenced his doubters by producing a tactical masterclass.

Osmand equalised with his first touch of the ball and then set up Duran for a goal. The Colombian then put his side 3-2 ahead after Orkun Kokcu had lashed in a long-range effort from fully 30 yards out. That opportunist strike ultimately clinched a much-needed, morale-boosting and richly deserved victory.

“It was designed to do exactly what it did,” said O’Neill afterwards. “We have been playing Sunday, Thursday, Sunday, Thursday, Wednesday. We have played something like nine games in 24 days. Some players need a little breather. You can get re-energised from coming on the field.”

Can Celtic, who take on Kilmarnock in the William Hill Premiership at Parkhead on Sunday, tap into the feelgood factor this performance and result have generated and maintain this sort of form in the weeks ahead?

Their defensive play throughout the 90 minutes and the offensive play during the last half an hour augur well for the future.

Cameron Carter-Vickers, who was pilloried for his error-strewn showing against Falkirk last weekend, was a colossus before making way for Dane Murray due to hamstring tightness. Is there really friction between the centre-half and his manager? It didn’t look like it.

Celtic failed to push on after their 7-1 mauling of Hearts in the league last month - but this fine triumph over a formidable side indicates that better times might lie ahead.

#2303. Free transfer signing is rediscovering his best form at Watford: Opinion

Watford have started this season poorly under Alessio Dionisi. Despite promising signs to start the year, Dionisi’s side have been stuck in a rut with led to talk of yet another managerial sacking from the Hornets.

Watford, who host Middlesbrough this weekend, need inspiration from somewhere as they look to climb away from the wrong end of the Championship table, and Myron Boadu could be providing exactly that.

The Dutch striker arrived at Vicarage Road on a free transfer in September after a difficult spell at PSV, but recent performances suggest he is beginning to rediscover the qualities that once made him such an exciting prospect.

His brace against Norwich City on October 31 was followed by an assist in the 1-0 victory over Lincoln City three days later. More importantly, these contributions have come alongside encouraging performances in other areas of his game.

Boadu is looking sharper, making intelligent runs and showing the work rate that made him so effective earlier in his career. There is a reason Boadu was once valued at more than €40m and attracted a €17m move to Monaco.

He scored 32 league goals in 64 appearances for AZ Alkmaar before enjoying another productive spell on loan at Bochum, where he registered nine goals in 19 Bundesliga appearances. Injuries and inconsistent opportunities subsequently disrupted his progress, but the underlying talent has never disappeared.

His early Watford numbers are encouraging. Across seven Championship appearances and 379 minutes, Boadu has scored twice and provided one assist, averaging approximately 0.7 goal involvements per 90 minutes, according to FotMob.

His 1.40 expected goals compared with two actual goals also suggests he is taking his opportunities well rather than relying on an unsustainable volume of chances.

There are encouraging signs beyond his attacking output, too. Boadu has registered five tackles, nine recoveries and 18 duels won, while recording 20 touches inside the opposition penalty area. These figures reflect a forward becoming increasingly involved in both Watford’s pressing game and their attacking transitions.

His developing partnership with Iker Bravo is another significant positive. Boadu’s understanding with the fellow forward appears to be growing, with their movement complementing one another without requiring extensive preparation.

The Norwich performance demonstrated their potential, while his contribution against Lincoln showed he can also influence games through his work without necessarily finding the net himself. Of course, seven appearances do not represent a complete revival, and Boadu still needs to deliver consistently over a longer period.

However, Watford cannot afford to overlook the encouraging signs. If he maintains this intensity and continues building his understanding with Bravo, the Hornets could have a valuable attacking weapon for the remainder of the campaign.

#2302. Auxerre vs. Paris FC - prediction, team news, lineups

By Seye Omidiora | 05 Nov 2026 07:12, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 07:14

Ten places separate Auxerre and Paris FC in the Ligue 1 table ahead of Saturday's matchday 10 fixture at Stade de l'Abbe-Deschamps.

Liam Rosenior's men could theoretically finish the weekend joint-top if they secure maximum points against the side with the division's worst defensive record and Paris Saint-Germain lose to Troyes.

Rosenior faced considerable criticism after his forgettable spell at Chelsea earlier this year, but the Englishman has Paris FC flying after nine matchdays.

Les Bleus sit fourth in the division with 17 points from a possible 27, level with third-placed Lyon, one behind second-placed Lille and just three adrift of local rivals PSG.

Much of their early-season success admittedly stems from their strong form at Stade Jean-Bouin, where they remain unbeaten after collecting 13 of a possible 15 points.

However, they have struggled to replicate those performances on their travels, losing half of their four away matches, including the last two against Lorient (1-0) and Lens (2-1).

Paris have not won away since early October's 3-2 success at Marseille, and a return of just four points from 12 on the road undermines their prospects of drawing level with PSG before Luis Enrique's men face Troyes later on Saturday.

Will Still's Auxerre have made a patchy start to the campaign, compounded by their failure to keep clean sheets in eight of their nine Ligue 1 fixtures.

The 14th-placed club's only shutout came in September's 1-0 victory over Nice, but Les Ajaistes have been unable to keep opponents at bay since.

That defensive weakness leaves Still's team vulnerable, and they enter Saturday's encounter having conceded 23 league goals, three more than bottom-placed Le Havre, further underlining their difficulties.

While their 13 goals exceed the totals of all but two bottom-half clubs, Brest (16) and Strasbourg (17), this weekend's hosts leave themselves with considerable work to do by conceding far too freely.

Nonetheless, eagle-eyed observers will note that Auxerre have conceded just five goals in front of their supporters while scoring as many themselves, offering encouragement ahead of matchday 10.

Given Paris' away struggles, Les Ajaistes, who have collected seven points from 12 on home soil, will back themselves to secure maximum points against Rosenior's high-flying side.

Lamfia Dioubate was dismissed after 11 minutes of the 5-1 defeat to Strasbourg last time out, leaving Auxerre without the midfielder.

While Sekou Fofana is touch-and-go due to a groin issue, Bryan Okoh, Remy Labeau Lascary and Assane Diousse are all sidelined for the hosts.

Cameron Archer leads Auxerre's scoring charts with six goals, three more than Danny Namaso, and the Englishman will aim to build on his prolific start to the campaign.

Paris, meanwhile, could be without Ligue 1's leading scorer Lassine Sinayoko, whose eight goals surpass everyone else's tally in the competition, while his five assists underline his dual threat.

Given that Sinayoko has scored half of Les Bleus' 16 league goals, the visitors may have to rely on alternative sources; only Maxime Lopez and Pablo Pagis have also scored more than once for Rosenior's side, leaving the manager short of proven match-winners.

Apart from Sinayoko, the capital club have several other injury doubts: Adama Camara (hamstring), Luca Koleosho (muscle), Jonathan Ikone (thigh) and Nhoa Sangui (muscle) could all miss out.

Nardi; Makosso, Tuanzebe, Akpa; Sy, Danois, Ahamada, Oppegard; Faivre, Namaso; Archer

Trapp; Traore, Chergui, Coppola, Otavio; Lopez, Lees Melou; Kebbal, Pagis, Simon; Sissoko

Auxerre's league-worst defensive record is concerning, but conceding just five goals across four home matches suggests that Saturday's assignment may be less straightforward than the table implies.

Paris have struggled to replicate their home performances on the road, and Sinayoko's potential absence could leave Rosenior's men settling for a point.

#2301. Martin O'Neill reveals Celtic masterplan behind game-changing subs

Celtic manager Martin O’Neill claimed his game-changing triple substitution was always part of the plan after the switches rescued his side’s Europa League hopes.

Subs Callum Osmand and Camilo Duran were the heroes as the Hoops came from behind to beat Besiktas 3-2 and get off the mark in the tournament at the fourth time of asking.

The Hoops struggled to get into the Besiktas penalty box before Duran, Osmand and Colby Donovan came on just after the hour mark.

James Forrest had forced the only save for the visiting goalkeeper before then and Celtic trailed 1-0 after Jordan Lotomba conceded a penalty on his first start.

But Osmand fired home with his first touch and set up Duran for the first of the Colombian’s two goals as Celtic emerged victorious in a thrilling finale.

Kasper Hogh and Sebastian Tounekti had offered little threat or safe possession of the ball and the difference in performance after the triple change was striking.

“It was designed to do exactly what it did,” O’Neill said of his selection choices.

“We have been playing Sunday, Thursday, Sunday, Thursday, Wednesday, whatever day it happens to be. We have played, I don’t know, nine games in 23 or 24 days.

“Some players needed a little breather and you can get re-energised from coming on the field.

“So I’m going to say that this was exactly our plan and those players who came on were going to come on at some stage or another in the game.”

The crowd played their part in a memorable final half-an-hour as Celtic secured a fourth win in 11 matches.

Fans had reacted angrily after a 2-0 defeat at Falkirk on Sunday, but the players got them back on their side with their reaction to going behind and when Duran netted seconds after Besiktas equalised in the 86th minute.

“They were a credit to the football club and you can’t get a bigger accolade than that,” O’Neill said. “They were really, really terrific.

“We could have caved in. We were doing fine then the penalty goes in, but we came roaring back. Substitutes made a big, big difference, got a lot of pace up front, but I was just delighted with the whole team.

“It might just give us a platform to charge on. It’s not going to be easy, but at least we can park the European events for a few weeks’ time, but this definitely gives us a chance.”

Arsenal have suffered a potential injury setback after Ezri Konsa was forced off during their 2-1 win over Slavia Prague in the Champions League on Wednesday evening.

Mikel Arteta confirmed the England defender was substituted because of a fitness issue, having appeared to struggle with his knee during the match.

The Arsenal manager confirmed the substitution was fitness-related rather than tactical, explaining that the defender had been playing a considerable amount of football recently.

"Obviously he's playing a lot," Arteta said after the match, via the Evening Standard. "He's played so many games in the last few weeks. He had some issues, and we decided to take him off."

Konsa's fitness will now be monitored, particularly with Arsenal continuing to juggle their domestic and European commitments.

There was better news regarding William Saliba, however, with the Frenchman returning to the matchday squad after missing the start of the season with a back injury.

West Brom missed the opportunity to move into the Championship’s top two after being held to a 1-1 draw by Southampton on Tuesday night. James Morrison’s side now turn their attention to this weekend’s clash with Millwall, with the Baggies preparing to host the Lions in a bid to strengthen their promotion push.

Ahead of this weekend’s clash, Morrison has been asked about his midfielder Ousmane Diakite, who has been in excellent form this season. The WBA boss was questioned on Diakite’s potential, specifically whether he is a Premier League player in the making, as reported by Steve Tudgay.

“He has Premier League attributes, when you look at him,” the former defender said. “It’s about consistency. We have seen Diakite in the Blackburn game, then the Preston game and now he has gone to that next level against West Ham.

Diakite has made an encouraging start to the 2026/27 Championship season, establishing himself as an important figure in the WBA midfield. The 26-year-old has featured in 14 league games, making 11 starts and averaging a FotMob rating of 7.22.

Diakite has shown composure in possession too, completing 88.5% of his 544 passes, including 86 line-breaking efforts. His recent performances have been particularly impressive, earning ratings of 7.8 against Southampton and Blackburn and 7.7 against Preston.

Isaac Price has also emerged as a key attacking threat, already contributing five goals this season. There is promising young talent in Rabby Nzingoula, Toby Collyer and Harry Whitwell, too, giving Morrison options for the future.

The group has helped West Brom make a strong start to the campaign, with its blend of experience and potential offering encouragement for a sustained promotion push.

#2297. Raul Jimenez reveals major four-word update that Wolves fans will love

Raul Jimenez has shared a fresh update on his ongoing recovery from an MCL injury, which will provide a serious boost to Wolves fans as the Mexican striker's return to action slowly but surely edges closer.

The good times are rolling right now at Wolves, who have ascended to second in the Championship behind league leaders Middlesbrough ahead of Saturday lunchtime's crunch trip to Wrexham following three successive victories over Watford, Cardiff and Bristol City.

It has, of course, relieved some much-needed pressure and scrutiny upon the shoulders of Cesar Peixoto, though the Portuguese boss is still not completely out of the woods and remains searching for the backing of some of the Old Gold faithful since his arrival from Gil Vicente to replace Rob Edwards over the summer.

Nonetheless, Wolves are beginning to truly find their rhythm and that's only made all the more impressive by the continued absence of Jimenez, who begun the campaign in strong form upon his emotional homecoming to the Black Country but has not kicked a ball in anger since September.

Jimenez has, however, revealed a new progression update relating to his recovery update, and the words used will provide delight to supporters, teammates and Peixoto alike as all at Molineux eagerly anticipate the veteran frontman's return.

The 36-year-old has been out of action since before the three-week international back in September after suffering a serious MCL injury 36 minutes into Wolves' 1-0 EFL Cup third-round defeat at Everton, in which many believed he shouldn't have been risked in the first place.

Peixoto, then, was left to rue his selection decision when the 130-cap Mexican international's injury turned out to be more severe than expected, forcing the Championship promotion hopefuls to initiate ligament surgery instead.

Surgery would see Jimenez ruled out of proceedings until January, representing a significant blow for Wolves in the wake of a lightning-fast reintroduction to life at Molineux after lighting up the World Cup with co-hosts Mexico by scoring three goals from four matches upon leaving Premier League outfit Fulham.

Wolves' patchy form following the international window amid defeats to both Middlesbrough and Bolton Wanderers ensured that his absence was sorely-felt, although others have stepped up to the mantle in recent weeks to allow the ex-Benfica and Fulham striker to focus entirely on his recovery process instead of rushing to get back sooner rather than later.

The experienced striker recorded his 500th career appearance against Bristol City on Wednesday

His latest update is, however, an extremely positive one, and it may leave the Old Gold faithful optimistic of an even earlier return to the pitch.

Jimenez has kept supporters in the loop with many social media posts and updates throughout his time on the treatment table, which will only be appreciated by the club's fans as they await his return.

He's been stepping up his recovery process in recent weeks, moving on to more intensive physical work as his condition continues to improve.

Jimenez has generally appeared to be in good spirits, too, and that's evidenced by his latest update.

Speaking via his Instagram story on Thursday, Jimenez wrote: "Making really good process 🔥💪🐺."

Just four words were required for an admission that will leave Wolves supporters delighted, with the caption accompanying a video of the goal-getting ace undertaking leg exercises in a bid to regain his strength and mobility.

It was either side of a series of stories posted to show Jimenez going through his paces with some arduous gym work, and he's certainly not prepared to let up or allow this injury to get the better of him as he continues to work harder and harder ahead of a return early in the new year.

Jimenez's fellow frontmen at Wolves have begun to find the back of the net with increasing regularity in recent times, and he'll only be hoping that trend continues until he's back on the pitch himself.

One of the main worries when Jimenez suffered his injury was about exactly who was going to come to the fore and provide the cutting-edge in the final third to compensate for his long-term absence after kicking off his second stint at Wolves with three goals from six Championship fixtures.

Thankfully, both Adam Armstrong and Sean Neave are finding form to ensure that Jimenez's absence doesn't end up derailing Wolves' campaign.

Armstrong, who has largely struggled for form since arriving from Southampton in February, looks to have rediscovered his goalscoring boots - which are esteemed at this level, of course - after recording his first strikes since August in back-to-back wins against Watford and Cardiff.

He wasn't on the scoresheet during Wolves' most recent midweek victory at Bristol City, where Newcastle United loanee Neave instead stepped up to the billing by appearing from the bench to score his first professional goals in style with a late brace in the final 10 minutes to seal a dramatic 2-1 away victory for the white-hot visitors.

#2296. Enzo Maresca now doubles down on his Gianluigi Donnarumma stance after latest Man City "mistake"

By Lewis Blain | 05 Nov 2026 07:47

Manchester City manager Enzo Maresca has defended Gianluigi Donnarumma once again following another costly mistake during their 3-2 win over RB Leipzig in the Champions League on Wednesday evening.

The Italian goalkeeper was criticised after allowing Ridle Baku's effort to beat him at the near post, adding to a growing list of errors this season.

However, Maresca has shown little indication that he intends to reconsider his first-choice goalkeeper ahead of Saturday's trip to face Nottingham Forest at the City Ground in the Premier League.

Donnarumma came under scrutiny after City's latest Champions League victory, having been unable to prevent Baku from equalising shortly after half-time.

The mistake followed previous errors against Aston Villa and Paris Saint-Germain, raising questions about the 27-year-old's consistency since arriving at the Etihad Stadium.

However, Maresca insisted that the incident was not a major concern, highlighting the goalkeeper's ability to produce important saves at the other end.

"It's not a concern, but it's a mistake," the City manager told reporters, via the BBC. "He recognises, he knows that it's a mistake. And now, in 48 hours, we have Nottingham Forest away, and we need to be focused on that."

Maresca was also asked whether the upcoming international break could provide an opportunity to reconsider his goalkeeping options, but again defended Donnarumma.

"I'm not thinking about the goalkeeper situation," he said. "We're talking about a mistake, but a few minutes before, he's done a save that any keeper in the world, they're not going to do that save!"

The City boss added that Donnarumma knows his performance was "not good" enough but believes he will improve in the coming weeks.

Donnarumma's recent errors are becoming difficult to ignore, particularly given his struggles with distribution and dealing with crosses into the penalty area.

However, Maresca's options behind the Italian are not particularly convincing, making a change difficult to justify.

City allowed James Trafford to leave during the summer, despite the Englishman's potential, and arguably made their own situation more complicated by not retaining a capable alternative.

Geronimo Rulli remains the back-up, and despite penalty heroics against Brighton & Hove Albion in the Carabao Cup, Maresca has already made clear that Donnarumma is his undisputed first choice.

The Italian's shot-stopping ability is also a major asset, as demonstrated by several outstanding interventions this season.

Despite the mistakes, it is difficult to envisage Maresca dropping Donnarumma anytime soon. The 6 foot 5 shot-stopper needs to improve his consistency, but City may have to persist with him given their current alternatives.

#2295. Sean Neave won Wolves the game against Bristol City, and he can do the same vs Wrexham: Opinion

Wolves staged an impressive fightback to defeat Bristol City on Wednesday night. Scott Twine put the Robins ahead, and it appeared they would be securing a valuable three-points against an in-form Wanderers side.

However, Cesar Peixoto would introduce Sean Neave in place of Fer Lopez on the 73rd minute, and the move changed the game. First, the Newcastle United loanee pounced on the rebound to level the scores, before meeting Ladislav Krejci’s header back across the box to slide home and win it for the Old Gold with a minute to spare.

The goals were Neave’s first and second for Wolves, and in senior football. The 19-year-old has offered some bright performances off the bench to date, and the traits that helped him win the Bristol City game could see him play a key role against Wrexham this Saturday too.

Neave is a raw and developing young striker. He had only played two times for Newcastle United’s first-team before joining Wolves, but a prolific record at U21 (19 goals in 36 games) and U18 (21 goals in 37 games) means he comes highly regarded from his parent club.

For the most part, Neave has been used as an impact player from the bench. He started and assisted in the win over Burnley and played the first 45 minutes against Portsmouth in the following game.

However, at the moment, it feels he is most effective as an impact sub, and the Bristol City game showed that. Having watched Neave closely at both Wolves and in his Newcastle United development, he has a great burst of pace to pounce on loose balls and run beyond the defensive line.

This was on show in his goals against Bristol City – first pouncing on a rebound before meeting a header at the back-post to tap in his second. In between those two goals, he got the jump on the Robins defence to run in behind before guiding an effort wide off the inside of his foot.

Neave’s sharpness, speed and quick movement will be a worry for Wrexham. A lot of their centre-backs are strong, but they do not have the speed to deal with Neave.

By either playing in balls for Neave to run onto or subbing him on late against tired legs, he can expose the lack of pace among the likes of Joe Worrall, Max Cleworth or Dom Hyam.

Game time is key for the development of all young players, and Neave arrived at Wolves knowing he would have to earn his opportunities. Raul Jimenez joined to lead the line, while Adam Armstrong has a fantastic record at Championship level.

However, Jimenez’s injury means Neave has found opportunities, and he’s taking them. The brace against Bristol City will hopefully be an exciting sign of what’s to come from the teenage striker – both over the course of this season and in his career.

Neave will be taking it one step at a time, but just as he did against the Robins, he can be a thorn in the side of Wrexham’s defence with his pace and movement on Saturday.

#2294. £300k Birmingham City man faces exit: Bolton Wanderers & Oxford United must react

As the winter months roll on, it won't be long until we're looking at how clubs across the EFL can improve their squads in the January transfer window.

Birmingham City will be looking to strengthen for what they hope will be a push towards securing a top-two finish and a place in the Premier League for the first time in 16 years.

But Chris Davies' side does have some deadwood that they'll need to rid themselves of, too, none more so than Scott Wright, who really hasn't settled at Birmingham at all since making the switch from Scottish giants Rangers in the summer of 2024 for around £300,000.

The 29-year-old has made just 24 appearances in the nearly two-and-a-half years he's spent at St Andrew's and was loaned out last January back to Scotland to see out the season with Dundee FC.

With just one one-minute cameo appearance in their EFL Cup to his name this season, he'll surely be angling for more game time, and Birmingham should be in a position to grant that.

Wright's career is a peculiar one. The former youth Scotland international was on the books at Rangers for two-and-a-half years, but never during his time at Ibrox was he a certified starter.

The winger was a very solid squad player during his time with the Gers, and a move south of the border for the first time in his career to a side expected to dominate League One in 2024/25 presented a good opportunity for him to take a more important role elsewhere.

That never came to fruition at Birmingham, though. Despite scoring a late winner on his debut against Wigan Athletic, injuries stopped Wright from playing much of a part in Chris Davies' side's record-breaking campaign.

Through AI, we'll rank the top 25 most intimidating football stadiums in British football, but which stadium comes out on top?

He played just 437 minutes of League One action, and a knee injury in just his third league start for the club in February 2025 not only curtailed his season, but also saw him out of the picture for the first half of last year's Championship return.

At that point, Birmingham had invested even more into the squad, and even though he did get off the bench for a few games back in January, it was always going to be a struggle to get into starting contention, especially as he wasn't while the Blues were in League One.

Wright needs a fresh start, and these two EFL sides should be on hand to give it to him.

Bolton Wanderers have had a fine start to life back in the Championship after a seven-year exile, sitting 17th with 17 points from 14 games and an away record which is amongst the best in the division.

Not many expected the Trotters to pack such a punch going forward, especially given the amount of quality they lost in those areas in the summer due to loanees returning to their parent club and the number of perceived top players they lost out on during the off-season.

Steven Schumacher has done well with the tools he's had, but it's evident that he still wants more, especially in the wide areas. Against Charlton Athletic on Tuesday night, he opted for Max Lott on the left wing, a 19-year-old they signed from Aston Villa's academy in October, for his first senior start for the club.

Five players, five careers. Name them from the clubs they played for.

Each day there are five current or former EFL players to identify. For each one you’ll see their senior clubs in the order they played for them. Loan spells are left out.

You get four guesses per player. Every wrong guess unlocks a clue: first their position, then their nationality, then their initials.

Get it first time for 4 points, then 3, 2 and 1. The best possible score is 20. A new set of players arrives at midnight.

Some saw that as a potential cry for some reinforcements in January. Thierry Gale aside, the Trotters don't look stunning on the wings. Sam Illing-Junior hasn't excelled, and Kyliane Dong hasn't done much to nail down a starting spot.

Additionally, both of those wingers are on loan at Bolton, so they won't be theirs in the future.

If Bolton can agree on a minimal deal to bring Wright to the Toughsheet Stadium, they'll have a permanent player entering their prime and with a big point to prove that he's good enough to play regular Championship games after not getting a sniff at Birmingham.

The likelihood is, though, that if Wright does remain in the EFL in January, he may find himself dropping down to the top of League One.

If Oxford United's transfer embargo, which forbade them from investing in Aaron Ramsey's squad this past summer, is lifted by January, as the higher-ups at the Kassam Stadium are confident it will be, they should have an eye on Wright to increase their attacking depth.

The Yellows will be in for a busy winter window if the embargo is lifted. Oxford have started the campaign brilliantly under Ramsey, who has taken to his first senior managerial role very well. After their win over Reading on Wednesday, they sit third in League One, three points off the top and with a game in hand.

Where do you think Scott Wright will end up in January? Let us know in the comments.

However, there are some worries about the lack of senior depth in the squad. Ramsey has been brilliant at utilising players from the academy, and it's led to 18-year-old Mo Missanga being one of the more exciting stars in the division.

But if they can bring in some experience out wide, that would be optimal, especially with Villa loanee Luka Lynch only at the club until January.

Wright would automatically be one of the more veteran attacking players in the Oxford side if they moved for him, and he could provide a good boost, if given the right minutes, as the Yellows look to maintain promotion form.
