#2293. Nottingham Forest vs Manchester City - prediction, team news, lineups

By Oliver Thomas | 05 Nov 2026 08:00, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 17:45

Second from bottom faces second from top in the Premier League on Saturday evening, as Nottingham Forest welcome Manchester City to the City Ground.

The two teams played out a 2-2 draw in their last meeting at the Etihad Stadium in March, with Elliot Anderson scoring a late equaliser for Forest before completing his big-money move to City in the summer.

Man City strengthened their hopes of securing automatic qualification for the Champions League last 16 with a 3-2 away victory over RB Leipzig on Wednesday, climbing into seventh place in the 36-team League Phase table at the halfway stage.

Manager Enzo Maresca has admitted that his team “struggled” in moments against Leipzig, but the German club’s nemesis Erling Haaland – wearing the captain’s armband – was on hand to slide home a 78th-minute match-winner for City and his 13th goal in just nine games against Die Roten Bullen.

The Citizens were made to work for their victory over Leipzig having previously suffered back-to-back Premier League defeats to Aston Villa and Brighton & Hove Albion, results that forced them to surrender top spot in the table to champions Arsenal.

City’s 24-game scoring streak in the Premier League was halted by a high-flying Seagulls side who battled to a narrow 1-0 victory at the Etihad last weekend, despite Maresca’s men registering 21 shots worth 1.92 expected goals (xG) and over 60% possession.

Not since February/March 2016 under Manuel Pellegrini have Man City lost three PL games in a row, and they head to the City Ground this weekend having won two of their previous four league visits (D1 L1) since Forest’s return to the top flight in 2022, most recently winning 2-1 in December last year.

Oliver Glasner has not made the desired impact as Nottingham Forest head coach, with his team winning only one of their opening nine Premier League games (D3 L5) and accumulating just a single point from their last 15 available, leaving them down in 19th place in the table.

Scoring goals has been the key issue for the Tricky Trees, who are the Premier League’s joint-lowest scorers (six) and have drawn a blank in five of their nine matches, despite registering the second-most shots per game in the division (16.9), only behind Manchester United (18.6).

A further 15 shots and just one on target in the 78th minute was recorded in last weekend’s 2-0 defeat at Brentford - another frustrating result that sparked a passionate post-match rant from Glasner, demanding his players to 'either buy in to changes or leave' to ensure that last season’s “s*** situation” does not repeat itself.

Forest’s hierarchy have reportedly welcomed Glasner’s strong words, but the real test is whether his misfiring squad will finally respond on the pitch. The Tricky Trees remains winless and goalless in five home fixtures this season, losing their last two to Coventry and Arsenal by a 1-0 scoreline.

Glasner now faces a daunting task against Man City, having lost four of his previous five league meetings with them (D1) during his time as Crystal Palace boss, conceding at least three goals in each defeat. However, Forest can draw confidence from their 1-0 home victory over City just two seasons ago, thanks to a late Callum Hudson-Odoi strike.

Nottingham Forest form (all competitions):

Nottingham Forest duo Nicolo Savona (knee) and Igor Jesus (calf) have both trained this week, but Saturday's game will come too soon for the former, while Nicolas Dominguez (muscle) is ruled out for around eight weeks.

Former Man City youngster Liam Delap has failed to score in his last five starts for Forest and is at risk of losing his centre-forward spot to Chris Wood, who is still waiting to start hist first league game of the season.

James McAtee – another ex-City player – has made a notable impression in a deeper midfield role where he is expected to continue, while Daniel Munoz – who has been managing a minor foot issue – and Ousmane Diomande will both be pushing for recalls in defence.

As for Man City, Maresca is not expected to make wholesale changes, but captain Ruben Dias and Josko Gvardiol are strong contenders to earn a recall in defence at the expense of Marc Guehi and Nico O'Reilly, who has been left out of the England squad due to fitness-related reasons.

Enzo Fernandez, who scored his first UCL goal for City in midweek, is the most likely midfielder to play alongside Anderson, though Ayyoub Bouaddi and Mateo Kovacic are two central options pushing to replace the Argentine.

Haaland – without a goal in his last four appearances against Forest – will lead the line once again and could be supported in attack by Iliman Ndiaye, Rayan Cherki and Jeremy Doku, the latter of whom began as a substitute against Leipzig.

Nottingham Forest possible starting lineup:

Sels; Cunha, Milenkovic, Murillo; Munoz, McAtee, Schlager, Williams; Ndoye, Gibbs-White; Delap

Manchester City possible starting lineup:

Donnarumma; Nunes, Khusanov, Dias, Gvardiol; Anderson, Fernandez; Ndiaye, Cherki, Doku; Haaland

Forest’s misfiring frontline is a growing concern for Glasner, but the hosts should take encouragement from Man City’s porous defence that has conceded 14 goals in their last seven games in all competitions.

A closely-contested battle could be in store this weekend, but we expect the Citizens to possess the clinical edge required to claim maximum points.

#2291. Huddersfield Town and Middlesbrough won’t believe what's happening at non-league side

It's not uncommon for ex-EFL stars to wind down their careers in non-league, but what's happening at Harborough Town, especially, will catch the eye of Middlesbrough, Huddersfield Town and neutral fans, too.

Harborough are one of the fastest-rising non-league sides right now, having only just joined Step 6 in the non-league ladder in 2010. This season, they're competing in the National League North (Step 2) for the first time in their history, after winning the Southern League Premier Central last year.

With the likes of Morecambe, Macclesfield FC and Hereford now competing with them in league action, the need for extra quality and some ex-EFL professionals was needed in the summer, and continues to be needed as the year goes on.

And that led Mitch Austin's side to form quite the peculiar strike partnership.

Harborough hit headlines ahead of their first season at National League North level by securing the arrival of Britt Assombalonga.

The 33-year-old is still technically Middlesbrough's record signing, having arrived from Nottingham Forest for £15 million back in the summer of 2017. However, Will Lankshear will be set to break that once the add-ons in his move from Tottenham Hotspur are activated.

It's safe to say that Lankshear will probably be better remembered in a Boro shirt, but Assombalonga wasn't exactly a dud. He scored double figures in three of his four Championship campaigns at the Riverside, including 15 in his debut year to help his side reach the play-offs.

After leaving Teesside in 2021, the 33-year-old bounced around Turkish clubs, in between a short stint at Watford, before playing a small part in Barnet's season last year in League Two, and has now dropped a couple more divisions to play for Harborough.

Now, though, he has some competition up top in the form of Kyle Hudlin, the former Huddersfield man and widely recognised as the tallest outfield player in England, standing at six-foot-nine.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

Hudlin signed for Huddersfield off the back of scoring in the National League play-off final for Solihull Moors in 2022. He made just 10 appearances for the Terriers, spending the majority of his time out on loan, including a stint at Newport County, where he scored six in 27 League Two appearances in 2024/25.

After a year in Vietnam, he's returned to England and has signed for Harborough.

Harborough have been on quite the meteoric rise, with four promotions in 15 years, the final three of which have come in the last five seasons.

Their first in the National League North has been steady, with Mitch Austin's side sitting two points above the bottom four in what is turning out to be yet another very competitive Step 2 campaign, after last season saw two sides relegated on 50 or more points.

Assombalonga has been in and out of the side, owing to a few niggling injuries, but has two goals and two assists in six league starts this season.

Harborough do have a small excuse for a slower start than in previous years, though, as they've gone through three qualifying rounds in the FA Cup to reach the First Round proper for just the second time in their history, and for the second time in three years.

Those who remember them from two years ago will know that they took League One side Reading all the way at the Select Car Leasing Stadium in the second round, losing in extra time after drawing 3-3 with them after 90 minutes.

This season, they travel to another third-tier side in the first round, with Mitch Austin's men heading to Leyton Orient. They'll be wanting to cause another scare, and even follow through on upsetting the O's this weekend.

It's not only Assombalonga and Hudlin who have EFL reps under their belts in this Harborough side. Behind them is former three-time League One champion Michael Jacobs, who has over 450 appearances in the Football League to his name and was playing at Northampton Town last year.

It's those players who will be called upon by the Bees to help them through difficult matchups, not only in the league against ex-league sides, but also in FA Cup bouts against teams three divisions higher.

Do you think Harborough Town could cause an FA Cup upset this weekend? Let us know in the comments.

With the amount of ex-EFL scoring power in the squad now, there'll be an expectation for the club to start firing more in the National League North, of which their 13 goals are a divisional low.

As and when they do, Huddersfield and Middlesbrough fans may keep a small glancing eye on whether it's either of their former pro players doing so. And if they both combine against Leyton Orient on Saturday, it's sure to get some traction amongst footballing circles, too.

#2287. Birmingham City fans will love Jude Bellingham's reaction to England news

Jude and Jobe Bellingham have received call-ups to the England senior squad under Thomas Tuchel for the first time as a duo, with the former paying tribute to their early-career spells at Birmingham City.

The Premier League and Championship will halt their respective campaigns after the upcoming weekend's round of fixtures, with the Three Lions in Nations League action against Croatia and Spain.

It has been a relatively successful year for England on the footballing stage, with a third-placed finish at the FIFA World Cup hosted in the United States, Canada and Mexico being the nation's best in the tournament since winning it in 1966.

Jude Bellingham was an integral part of the side's run to the semi-finals and has continued to impress in the four Nations League matches during the previous international window.

Many of the high-profile names at Tuchel's disposal can credit spells at various EFL clubs for aiding their eventual development, including Ballon d'Or winner, Harry Kane.

Unsurprisingly, Bellingham was quick to reference his boyhood club yet again in a proud family milestone.

A reunion with Chris Wood has been debated amongst Bluenoses ahead of the January window

Jude and Jobe Bellingham are the most high-profile names to have graduated from Birmingham's Wast Hills academy setup this century, with the former becoming the club's youngest-ever player and goalscorer seven years ago before initially moving to Borussia Dortmund.

The West Midlands outfit then opted to retire the '22' shirt to help create a legacy, and that has only grown stronger through the 23-year-old's current exploits with Real Madrid and England.

Jobe, 21, played 26 times for Blues between 2022 and 2023 before moving to Sunderland for £3m, and after the Black Cats' Premier League return, also signed for Dortmund in a £27.8m deal.

He has since shone for the Bundesliga giants, and after impressing domestically and in the UEFA Champions League, the 12-time Under-21s international earned his first senior Three Lions call-up on Friday.

Such feats were marked by his older brother on social media, who posted a picture of them sporting Birmingham colours during their academy days.

During the early years of his career, Jobe has often been compared to his older brother, despite rightfully wanting to create his own story on the pitch.

That has clearly been the case given his rave reviews at Dortmund, whilst he also racked up over 100 Championship appearances for Birmingham and Sunderland.

Like Jude, the Stourbridge native has never lost sight of the impact Blues staff had in order for him to flourish at such a young age.

“I’m so grateful for everything they’ve (Birmingham) done for me over many years, since I was really young," he said shortly after leaving B9.

Blues fans, how proud are you of Jude and Jobe Bellingham's exploits? Comment below!

“At Birmingham, a lot of the academy coaches helped loads, just with my understanding of the game. I think they’ve made me a better person, I believe. There’s also a lot of players in the first team who helped me grow into the first-team environment.

Whilst they have their own career paths to focus on, both Jude and Jobe will be loving the developments at St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park right now, which is a stark contrast to how the club was run during their respective breakthrough years.

Knighthead's plans for the Sports Quarter and Powerhouse stadium have seen Jude involved in promotional videos, whilst he was also present at the pre-season friendly against FC Barcelona.

On the pitch, Chris Davies' side are doing their utmost to end a long-standing Premier League exile, as Blues sit third in the Championship having lost just two of their first 14 games.

Joyous feelings will be reciprocated by those associated with the club for the progress made by the duo, who, in turn, will hope to see the club back in the top-flight very soon.

#2286. Michael Carrick reacts to Man Utd boos after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick admitted Manchester United’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur was a painful result, after his side failed to protect the lead against 10-man Tottenham at Old Trafford.

Manchester United looked set to claim three points when Bryan Mbeumo scored in the second half off Cunha’s low cross, but Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser in injury time left Carrick facing more questions on United’s performance and his future.

Speaking to BBC Match of the Day, the United manager felt his players improved after the break but failed to handle the situation when victory was within reach.

“It was a mixed game. We found it tough to start with. For a number of reasons, we were not quite there. You could see it was a challenging time,” Carrick said.

“But the lads grew into the game, in the second half I thought we were better in everything. To get the game as we wanted it, in such a good position – we should see it through. We have to manage those situations.”

United have now conceded two late goals that have cost them valuable points, something Carrick believes has made their position look worse than it might have otherwise.

Asked whether a lack of confidence had contributed to United’s slow start, he disagreed but admitted matches can quickly slip away from his side.

The boos at half-time and full-time also clearly affected him.

“It’s not nice, it hurts a lot because I care about this club. But I understand the disappointment, the reality is what it is, we need to get back to winning games.”

#2285. Man City lineup vs. Nottingham Forest: Predicted XI for Premier League clash

By Oliver Thomas | 05 Nov 2026 08:05, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 15:47

Manchester City manager Enzo Maresca will weigh up making a couple of alterations to his starting lineup for Saturday’s Premier League clash with Nottingham Forest at the City Ground.

The Citizens bounced back from their 1-0 defeat to Brighton in the top flight with a 3-2 away success over RB Leipzig in the Champions League on Wednesday, with Erling Haaland netting a late match-winner.

Elliot Anderson provided a superb assist for Haaland and the former Forest midfielder is now preparing to play against the team he left in a £116m summer deal for the first time.

Enzo Fernandez was on the scoresheet against Leipzig and may continue alongside Anderson, though Ayyoub Bouaddi and Mateo Kovacic are two central options pushing for a recall.

Rayan Cherki, who scored the winning goal in City’s 2-1 triumph at Forest last season, will likely retain his starting spot in the number 10 role behind Haaland up front, but Phil Foden may revert to the substitutes’ bench.

Indeed, Iliman Ndiaye may take Foden’s place on the right flank, which would open the door for Jeremy Doku to earn a recall on the left side of attack.

Ruben Dias was benched for the first time this season in either the Champions League or Premier League on Wednesday, but City’s captain is expected to return this weekend and may start alongside Abdukodir Khusanov at centre-back, forcing Marc Guehi to drop down to the bench.

Josko Gvardiol could return at left-back at the expense of Nico O'Reilly who has been left out of the England squad for fitness-related reasons, but Matheus Nunes and Gianluigi Donnarumma are likely to start again at right-back and in goal respectively; Donnarumma’s starting spot seems safe for now despite an error-strewn run of form.

Manchester City possible starting lineup:

Donnarumma; Nunes, Khusanov, Dias, Gvardiol; Anderson, Fernandez; Ndiaye, Cherki, Doku; Haaland

#2284. Bryan Mbeumo reacts to Man Utd draw with Tottenham

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bryan Mbeumo admitted Manchester United have work to do after their frustrating 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, despite scoring a superb second-half goal at Old Trafford.

The Cameroon international put Manchester United ahead in the 73rd minute, finishing in the far corner after Matheus Cunha created the opportunity from a low cross. However, Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory, even though Tottenham had been reduced to 10 men following Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card.

Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2).

3 – Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2). Disadvantage? pic.twitter.com/DuSpvymZfu

Speaking to Sky Sports after the game, Mbeumo acknowledged that United had taken too long to find their rhythm.

“We took time to get into the game, but we played well in the second half. In the first half, that wasn’t us, we had to change. We tried to get at them,” he said.

Mbeumo recognised the threat Tottenham posed and admitted the result was disappointing, particularly given United’s position in the game before Solanke’s late goal.

“We know Spurs have good players who can be dangerous. It’s a bit disappointing, we need to work on things.”

United have struggled for consistency in the opening weeks of the season, and dropping points after taking the lead will do little to ease the pressure on Carrick. Mbeumo, however, insisted the team must look ahead rather than dwell on the setback.

“We need to keep going forward, as we have a lot of games.”

Michael Carrick was pleased with Mbeumo’s contribution, highlighting both the quality of his finish and the different roles he has played since joining United.

“Bryan’s done great. It was a fantastic finish again; he’s obviously played different roles for us but we know what he offers,” the United manager said.

“He’s got good quality and obviously the more goals he scores, the better. He’s doing really well for us.”

Mbeumo’s goal was one of the few bright moments in another difficult afternoon for United. Carrick’s team must now find a way to turn things around from here after just six points from six games.

Manchester United have come under fresh criticism after their disappointing 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, with Sky Sports pundits Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp questioning the team’s performance and the challenges facing Michael Carrick.

Manchester United looked on course for a much-needed victory at Old Trafford after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half. However, despite Tottenham being reduced to 10 men, Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser denied Carrick’s side all three points.

Gary Neville felt Tottenham deserved the points, pointing to Solanke’s late goal and his impact as Tottenham captain.

“Tottenham deserved a point. That was a really important moment for Solanke. He’d missed some big chances and was captain for the first time,” Neville said on Sky Sports.

“They were the better team by far. If they’d left without a point, it would have been terrible for them.”

The former United defender also expressed concern about what the result means for Carrick, who faces increasing pressure to improve performances and results.

“Their energy levels are not good enough, and they look loose going forward; there was no real structure.”

Redknapp added that Carrick cannot fix every issue alone, insisting the manager needs backing to improve the squad.

“The squad is not good or strong enough, and Michael [Carrick] needs help in that respect.”

#2280. ‘Embarrassing themselves!’ Jamie Carragher hits back at Rio Ferdinand over Cristiano Ronaldo defence

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher has taken aim at Rio Ferdinand for defending Cristiano Ronaldo following the Portuguese forward’s latest controversy with his national team.

Ronaldo has found himself at the centre of a growing dispute after abruptly leaving Portugal’s national team camp following a reported disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus. The incident has prompted strong reactions from former teammates and pundits, with Ferdinand firmly siding with the Al-Nassr star.

Jamie Carragher, however, believes Ronaldo’s reputation should not shield him from criticism when his behaviour becomes difficult to defend.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Cristiano Ronaldo at Manchester United between 2003 and 2009, criticised Jorge Jesus for the way he handled the situation.

Speaking on his Rio Presents podcast, the former United captain criticised Jesus for not showing respect to Cristiano Ronaldo.

“Cristiano Ronaldo was put in a very bad situation and was treated in an inappropriate way. He has an enormous history in football. Jorge Jesus did not deal with Ronaldo well and chose to treat him in that way, but I cannot find any explanation for it,” Ferdinand said.

Despite Jesus being Portugal’s manager, Ferdinand questioned his standing, comparing him with the likes of Sir Alex Ferguson and Pep Guardiola. To say the least, Ferdinand only embarrassed himself doing that.

Jamie Carragher saw the argument very differently and rightfully so.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily, the former Liverpool defender suggested that Ferdinand’s friendship with Ronaldo was influencing his reaction to the incident.

“People think it’s good to be close to people in the game, but this is where it becomes a problem,” Carragher explained.

He then called out both Ferdinand and broadcaster Piers Morgan, who has also been vocal in his support of Ronaldo.

“People like Piers Morgan and Rio Ferdinand, who are big people in the media whether you like them or not, are embarrassing themselves with the nonsense that they’re coming out with because they know Ronaldo. You can know someone and try to look after them, but you cannot defend the indefensible.”

Carragher believes Ronaldo’s achievements should not put the forward beyond criticism, particularly when his actions involve his national team and its management.

Gary Neville, who also played with Cristiano Ronaldo, has questioned Ronaldo’s handling of the situation, despite his longstanding affection for his former Manchester United teammate.

Neville argued that Ronaldo’s public response had made matters worse and the forward needed to accept criticism rather than expect others to defend him.

“With Cristiano, it’s difficult because I love him, but I can’t watch what happened at United in his second period and then see him make a statement like that without saying something. He has to be pulled up on that and told that it isn’t acceptable,” Neville said.

He also suggested Ronaldo’s extraordinary success, wealth and influence could have contributed to a sense that he was beyond challenge.

“When you have a billion pounds in the bank, the achievements he has had and the power he has, you feel you’re unbreakable, you feel you’re unstoppable,” Neville added.

Despite his criticism, Neville does not believe the episode will define Ronaldo’s legacy permanently. He expects the attention to fade once the forward retires, allowing his achievements on the pitch to take centre stage again.

#2279. Man Utd dealt hammer blow in pursuit of 'dream' Michael Carrick replacement as fresh update emerges

By Lewis Blain | 05 Nov 2026 08:10

Manchester United have suffered a major setback in their pursuit of top managerial target Unai Emery after Aston Villa confirmed positive discussions over a new contract with the Spaniard.

The Villa boss had been identified as a 'dream' target to replace Michael Carrick at Old Trafford, but the Midlands outfit are increasingly confident of keeping him beyond his current agreement.

The development could leave the Red Devils hierarchy having to reconsider their managerial options as uncertainty over Carrick's position continues.

In a strange move, Aston Villa announced on social media that they have held positive discussions with Emery about extending his contract beyond 2029.

The 54-year-old signed a five-year agreement in 2024, but a meeting with chairman Nassef Sawiris, president of football operations Roberto Olabe and director of football operations Damian Vidagany has strengthened the club's belief that he will commit his future to Villa Park.

In a statement, Villa said: "The discussions focused on jointly planning the club's long-term strategic direction, with positive discussions establishing a clear commitment beyond 2029 for the joint development of the club under the sporting leadership of Unai Emery."

Aston Villa held an important working meeting today to plan the club's future and lay the foundations for its long-term sporting and institutional project. The meeting brought together Nassef Sawiris, the club's Chairman and Unai Emery alongside President of Football Operations Roberto Olabe and Director of Football Operations Damián Vidagany. The discussions focused on jointly planning the club's long-term strategic direction, with positive discussions establishing a clear commitment beyond 2029 for the joint development of the club under the sporting leadership of Unai Emery based on the shared ambitions and common goal to reach excellency and keep growing during the next years. The objective is clear: to build an ambitious, competitive Aston Villa capable of taking on the biggest challenges and achievements.

The announcement follows reports that United regard Emery as their preferred candidate should they decide to part ways with Carrick. INEOS are understood to admire the Spaniard's achievements, including securing Champions League qualification twice and winning the Europa League with Villa.

However, convincing him to leave the project he has built in the Midlands was always going to be difficult, particularly given the significant compensation United would need to pay.

United's pursuit of Emery now looks increasingly complicated, leaving the club with fewer obvious managerial alternatives.

Carrick has received criticism following a difficult run of results, but his side's 3-2 comeback victory over AS Roma in midweek will certainly help his cause.

Eddie Howe and Julian Nagelsmann have also been discussed as potential replacements, although there are different preferences within United's recruitment structure.

With Emery seemingly committed to Villa and no straightforward appointment available, United may have little choice but to give Carrick more time.

That does not mean the former Red Devils midfielder has secured his long-term future, but the lack of clear-cut alternatives could extend his spell in the Old Trafford dugout.

For now, a positive run of results is likely to be the most important factor in determining whether Carrick can convince United's hierarchy that he deserves to remain in charge.

#2278. Is Mainoo-Tielemans the right midfield option for Man Utd?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United drew 1-1 at home to Tottenham despite having an extra man advantage. The Reds saw Mbeumo put them in front, but Solanke scored a late leveller in injury time after Bentancur was shown a red card for headbutting Luke Shaw.

Manchester United’s worrisome season continues after accumulating just six points off the first six games, their first start in 40 years. But more than the results, what worries Manchester United is their midfield pairing.

United spent massively on their midfield rebuild and somehow can’t find the best combination in the midfield. Michael Carrick has gone with the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership, but they have failed to control the midfield. Individually, they both come with decent numbers, but collectively they fall short.

Mainoo and Tielemans struggled against Tottenham while closing the midfield and at times showed a lack of energy. On the day when nothing clicked for United, the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership particularly raised eyebrows.

Gary Neville has also raised serious concerns about Manchester United’s Mainoo-Tielemans partnership.

Neville was particularly unimpressed by the midfield balance, arguing that the problems went beyond one disappointing performance.

The former United captain stopped short of ruling out the combination altogether, but he suggested he has serious doubts about its suitability.

“Tielemans and Mainoo, I’m not calling time on it, that’s probably too drastic, but I’ve seen enough of it to think that’s not a midfield that’s dynamic enough to play at this football club in the Premier League.”

Former Spurs midfielder Redknapp felt United struggled to connect their midfield with the rest of the team during the opening stages.

He added: “I didn’t see enough connections in that midfield in the first hour. Baleba came on and did OK and showed a bit more.

“They look devoid of any confidence, any idea, any patterns of play, playing out from the back. Tottenham were much better than them in every single department.”

While Carlos Baleba was a particular highlight on the day, he showed great potential when brought on. He brought a new sense of energy in the midfield and sent a few balls over the top for a couple of quick counterattacks as well.

Redknapp also pointed to the physical demands of modern Premier League football, arguing that United cannot afford to be outworked by their opponents.

Tottenham ran a staggering 5 km more than Manchester United, something Redknapp felt was a pattern.

"Spurs ran 5km more than Man United… it's like that EVERY GAME!"Jamie Redknapp is less than impressed with the lack of ground covered this season by Manchester United 😬 pic.twitter.com/BxWkFuyGJ1

“There is a pattern that I don’t really like when I watch teams, and that’s when every time the stats don’t lie and the other team runs more than you.

“I feel in Premier League football right now there are a couple of non-negotiables, and one is you have to run more than the other team if you want to win or at least get close enough. Tonight Spurs ran 5km more than Manchester United.”

He believes Tottenham’s pressing exposed the lack of pace in United’s midfield, making it difficult for the hosts to move the ball safely through the centre.

“In terms of how they played today, I think they have an issue with Tielemans and Mainoo in midfield. I don’t see enough energy in there. Baleba will add a lot and will give them a tremendous amount of energy but right now, when things aren’t going well… and Tottenham knew it!

“Every time United played the ball out from the back they pounced. They pressed them because they knew United don’t have the pace and speed in midfield to get away.”

“In isolation, one of them is absolutely fine, the two of them together doesn’t work for me. They haven’t got the pace or the energy to get around the pitch for 90 minutes.”

#2277. Spurs have one Preston North End transfer clause that already looks doomed

Preston North End's 12-season stay in the Championship is in serious jeopardy based on their early season form.

The Lilywhites were long-term stayers in the second tier the last time they were here, keeping themselves in from 2000 until 2011 with some near promotion misses along the way, before dropping into League One.

Since 2015, PNE have been back in the Championship, mostly being a mid-table team, albeit with some brief flirtations with the top six and also the drop zone, and there was hope over the summer that a switch in transfer business would unlock a new level for them.

North End are usually quite frugal with their transfer budget, cherry-picking some younh, high-quality Premier League loanees and opting to recruit permanent players on cheaper fees or free transfers.

There was much evidence of that last summer when Alfie Devine, Lewis Dobbin, Daniel Jebbison and Harrison Armstrong arrived on loan, whilst they saved paying fees until January of last season, when Odel Offiah, Andrew Moran and Callum Lang all arrived at Deepdale.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

For whatever reason though, that tact changed this past summer, with around £15 million spent on new faces and over £5 million recouped on Milutin Osmajic and Ben Whiteman's sales.

And £6 million of that windfall spent was making the loan signing of Devine a permanent one, which came as a surprise to many North End fans, given the interest surrounding his services.

Celtic, Southampton and Wolves were among the clubs that were keen on taking Devine from Spurs, but even after the initial deadline was missed to seal a deal with the playmaker, PNE were able to convince the 22-year-old that Deepdale was the best place for his future.

Devine was once the youngest player and scorer for Tottenham when he featured in an FA Cup clash with Marine in 2021, but he had to go out on several loans to develop, with stints taken in at Port Vale, Plymouth Argyle and Belgian outfit Westerlo before ending up at PNE.

And with Tottenham being a big-spending club looking to turn around their fortunes under Roberto De Zerbi, there was no room for Devine in his midfield for 2026-27, leading to the decision to let him move on permanently for a decent fee, much like they did with Will Lankshear and Ash Phillips when selling the pair to Middlesbrough.

Spurs hold extra clauses when it comes to Lankshear and Phillips, which means they have a vested interest in their futures and will want Boro in the Premier League, as well as matching rights on the pair, which means they are given the chance to match any accepted bid that comes in for their services - it's not quite like that with Devine, but there's still a sell-on clause in there.

The Daily Mail reported at the time of the deal that Tottenham had negotiated a 20 per cent sell-on clause with PNE for Devine, which is based on any profit the Lancashire outfit end up making on Devine - as it stands though, that could be in jeopardy.

The reason why Devine was so in-demand during the summer was his form for North End, with eight goals scored from midfield and six assists registered.

But he's showed nothing like that kind of ability for PNE in the 2026-27 season, scoring just one goal and notching one assist, with the Lilywhites looking like they're in big relegation trouble already.

Ahead of their 15th match of the Championship season against Southampton, North End have just five points on the board and are nine points from safety, which whilst not confirming relegation by any stretch of the imagination, they'll need to go some way to overturn the form they've showed so far.

Paul Heckingbottom's early sacking and club icon Graham Alexander coming into replace him hasn't helped results whatsoever, and Devine is yet to show what he's really capable of - with the general consensus of the fanbase being that he's missing the link-up with now-Southampton forward Dobbin that he shared last season.

Clubs in the 2026–27 Sky Bet Championship, listed alphabetically.

#2276. One lesson Senne Lammens learned after Tottenham’s late equaliser

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Senne Lammens believes Manchester United must learn to finish games off after their late collapse against Tottenham Hotspur.

The result left the goalkeeper frustrated at Old Trafford despite leading the game and with a player more on the field.

Manchester United looked set for three points after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half, but Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory.

Speaking to MUTV, Senne Lammens admitted the team had struggled early on but had done enough after the break to put themselves in a winning position.

“Football can be a bit cruel at times,” the Belgian goalkeeper said. “There are a lot of ups and downs, especially in a game like that.”

Senne Lammens felt United failed to take advantage of Tottenham’s red card, allowing the visitors too much space to find a late equaliser.

“We’ve won a lot of games against 10 men [in the past], but you also know there can be a tricky situation when you’re playing against 10 men, because they have nothing to lose and they know that.”

He added that United should have scored again to put the result beyond doubt or, at the very least, prevented Tottenham from creating further chances.

“I think that’s what we did wrong. We gave them a little bit too much breathing room to create those chances, and then one ball has to go right for them, one bounce has to go their way, and they can score like that.”

The goalkeeper also pointed to similarities with United’s earlier disappointment against Everton, where points slipped away late in the match.

“You feel like those games just slip away from you, and there are one or two situations where you don’t really have control,” he said.

“I think there’s a lot to learn from today. It wasn’t all negative, not at all, but especially the way it ended left a very bad feeling.”

“I think the easy answer is that we have to be able to kill the game faster and not put ourselves in that position to give them a chance.”

Despite the frustration, Lammens said there were positives to take from the performance. However, the late goal had left a bitter feeling, and United now need to turn those lessons into better results.

#2275. Manchester United defence: Why Carrick must trust Yoro and Heaven

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United endured another embarrassing draw against 10-man Tottenham after failing to register their second win despite bringing in a plethora of players.

Manchester United invested heavily last season in their attacking options, bringing in Bryan Mbeumo and Matheus Cunha.

This season, they overhauled their midfield. United spent massively on Carlos Baleba, Youri Tielemans and Andrey Santos. Despite spending massively, they have failed to produce the control they would have expected in the midfield. The lack of control was evident in the recent Tottenham game, where Mainoo-Tielemans partnership failed to control the game collectively.

The midfield question was covered in our recent report as well, highlighting a lack of control and carried comments from Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp.

The bigger question is when United will strengthen their defence. As per our previous report as well, Manchester United are planning to overhaul their defence in the upcoming transfer windows.

United were heavily linked with several left backs but eventually failed to bring any one. The situation left Luke Shaw as the only senior option in the position, while Patrick Dorgu is used upfront by Michael Carrick.

Harry Amass is another option for Michael Carrick, but he is only here because of a failed transfer move. He has been rarely used at left back as Dalot and Mazraoui get the nod in the position ahead of him.

The situation at right-back isn’t all good either. Diogo Dalot is the first option there, but he rarely provides the assurance that puts Michael Carrick at ease. Noussair Mazraoui is the second choice at right-back, but his attacking numbers aren’t great either.

Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez form up United’s centre-back pairing while Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven are used all over the defensive line in rotation when needed. Yoro was used at right-back against Tottenham while Heaven arrived as left-back towards the end of the game.

Manchester United’s season hasn’t been defensively strong or anywhere near being good. They have conceded goals in all Premier League games and only kept one clean sheet against Sabah in the Champions League.

Lisandro Martinez has come under scrutiny after errors on four occasions. Against Ipswich Town, Manchester United won 5-2, but his error resulted in Ipswich’s second goal.

His own goal against Fulham gave away two points at Craven Cottage in the 1-1 draw. Another error led to Solanke’s goal against Tottenham, when he took his eyes off the ball.

His injury concerns are another aspect, and maybe Michael Carrick needs to make a decision.

Manchester United’s success depended heavily on youth over the years. When pundits questioned them, Sir Alex Ferguson gave them the chance to prove them wrong. Alan Hansen made the famous quote “You can’t win anything with kids” on the BBC’s Match of the Day on August 19, 1995. United did exactly that, played youth and went on to win everything.

Michael Carrick needs to do exactly that. He needs to trust youth and allow them to make mistakes. Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven arrived as massive potentials, but they haven’t been given the license to express themselves, without fearing for their places. Both the youngsters have rarely started together or in a stretch of games. It is maybe time for them to take their chances.

Michael Carrick needs to keep Harry Maguire as a senior experienced figure around them. There is no doubt over Martinez’s ability, but he is not the answer to United’s long-term problem. United need to blend youth with experience as their identity moving forward. Lisandro Martinez perhaps isn’t the answer to United’s problem.

A defensive revival expected in the upcoming transfer windows needs to happen now. It must happen with chances to the Youth. Michael Carrick needs to be brave enough and throw the youngsters in the mix. What it will bring is a long-term solution. They need to experience the errors and pressures that will shape them in football.

Challinor was named as Henrik Pedersen's replacement at Hillsborough this week, and while his immediate focus will be on improving Wednesday's form in League One, he may already have one eye on the January transfer window.

Wednesday made a total of 18 new signings during the summer transfer window as part of their rebuild under new owner David Storch, and Davis Keillor-Dunn was one of their standout additions, with the forward arriving on loan from Wrexham until January.

After scoring 35 goals in 78 games during a productive spell at League One side Barnsley, Keillor-Dunn joined Championship outfit Wrexham in January for a fee of around £2 million, but he made just seven substitute appearances for the Red Dragons in the second half of last season.

However, Wednesday may be fearful of losing Keillor-Dunn in January after Wrexham striker Sam Smith was ruled out for the rest of the season with an ACL injury, meaning manager Phil Parkinson could look to bring the 29-year-old back to South Wales to provide extra cover in the attacking areas.

"I suppose we'll just have to wait and see on that one.

With Louie Barry out injured, Keillor-Dunn has been Wednesday's main attacking threat in recent weeks, so it would certainly be a major blow if they were unable to extend his loan beyond January.

Wrexham could opt to recall Keillor-Dunn in January after Smith's season-ending injury, but he has been deemed surplus to requirements for much of his time at the SToK Cae Ras, so it seems more likely that the Red Dragons will enter the market for a replacement.

#2268. Why must Manchester United move beyond the Glazers?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Glazers took control of Manchester United in 2005. That takeover has since caused so many disruptions and protests by the fans over massive debts and insufficient backing of the team in the transfer market, despite successful periods in the Premier League and Europe.

Now Manchester United need to think about who should control the club in the future. Laurie Whitwell has now shared his views on why Manchester United need an ownership change. He simply wants the Glazers gone from Old Trafford. However, he also recognizes that replacing the current ownership doesn’t automatically mean things will improve for United.

The journalist believes the change in ownership has had a lasting effect on United, with the club still feeling its consequences today. A fresh start might be welcome, but the identity of the next owners would matter just as much as the departure of the existing ones.

“Personally, I would like to see the Glazers gone from Manchester United,” Laurie Whitwell said on The Rest is Football podcast with Gary Lineker.

“That change, you know, just really affected and still impacts United now.”

The desire for different ownership is one thing, but finding people capable of taking the club forward is another.

Whitwell admitted there is no perfect answer when it comes to who should take charge.

That leaves United facing a difficult choice. A sale could offer the chance to change how the club is run, but simply bringing in a new owner would not guarantee better decisions or a brighter future.

Whitwell also raised the idea of United supporters having a greater say in the club’s ownership and decisions, much like the Barcelona model where fans own the club itself. However, he highlighted that the Barcelona model isn’t ideal either and has its own shortcomings.

Changing the names at the top would only be one part of the change. The club would also need an ownership structure capable of making sound decisions and providing a direction.

The issue of ownership goes beyond whether the Glazers should stay or leave. Manchester United needs to find the right people and the right model to secure the club’s future. Getting that decision wrong could leave them facing the same circus the fans are enduring under the Glazers.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Argentinian defender has also apologized to the United higher ups as well over his comments, as shared by Fabrizio Romano.

Lisandro Martinez has held discussions with Jason Wilcox and Michael Carrick after speaking out following United’s 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid.

Fabrizio Romano has now confirmed that United consider the matter resolved for now and expect Martinez to remain available for selection.

#2266. Boreham Wood vs Wigan Athletic - prediction, team news, lineups

By Carter White | 05 Nov 2026 09:10

Aiming to continue their exceptional winning run, National League pacesetters Boreham Wood welcome League One's Wigan Athletic to Mangata Developments Stadium Meadow Park in the first round of the FA Cup on Saturday afternoon.

The Wood secured a dramatic last-minute success over Hartlepool United last time out in Hertfordshire, whilst the Latics played out a goalless draw at home with fellow strugglers Mansfield Town.

Losing in the National League playoff final to Rochdale at Wembley Stadium last season, Boreham Wood are in no mood to endure that lottery once again, with Saturday's hosts cruising towards a title success in the fifth tier.

Remarkably, the Wood have enjoyed a 16-game unbeaten start (W14 D2) to their league campaign, winning 14 of their past 15 fifth-tier contests since a 3-3 draw with Tamworth on the opening day.

Across National League, FA Cup qualifying and National League Cup action, Luke Garrard's troops are currently in the midst of a nine-game winning streak, however, that run was in danger of halting last time out.

The runaway league leaders found themselves 3-2 down to relegation-threatened Hartlepool at Meadow Park last weekend, when late strikes from Zak Brunt and talisman Matt Rush snatched three points for the hosts.

Netting the 92nd-minute winner off the bench, 25-year-old Rush has scored 10 goals in as many National League appearances and leads the division's Golden Boot race, whilst Boreham Wood are 12 points clear of second-placed Harrogate Town in the table.

Wigan Athletic return to action in the competition that provided them with their only major trophy, with Saturday's visitors lifting the FA Cup at Wembley in 2013 after an added-time Ben Watson stunner downed Manchester City in the final.

Thirteen years on, the Latics are struggling at the foot of the third division of English football, winning just three of their opening 14 league contests, sitting in 21st position in the League One standings.

Since three consecutive defeats by Oxford United (1-2), Huddersfield Town (1-2) and Leicester City (0-3), Gary Caldwell's men have picked up four points across matches against whipping boys Bromley (3-2) and Mansfield (0-0).

A worrying statistic ahead of this weekend's trip to Meadow Park, Wigan have failed to win any of their first seven away battles (D1 L6) this term and possess the worst record on the road in the third tier, collecting just a single point on their troublesome travels.

On a more positive note, the Latics enjoyed a respectable run in last season's FA Cup, beating Hemel Hempstead, Barrow and Championship side Preston North End before eventually falling to Premier League champions Arsenal at the Emirates Stadium in the fourth round.

After making a goalscoring impact off the bench at the expense of Hartlepool last weekend, Boreham Wood's leading striker Rush should start this FA Cup clash.

The 25-year-old is likely to replace Jay Bird at the focal point of attack, despite the Salford City loanee scoring six goals in 11 league appearances for the Wood.

Wigan are sweating over the fitness of veteran striker Scott Hogan, who was forced off during the first half of the stalemate with Mansfield earlier in the week.

The absence of the former Birmingham City attacker would likely lead to Christian Saydee earning a starting spot for the trip to Meadow Park.

Adding to the forward issues for the visitors, Dara Costelloe is expected to be sidelined for up to four weeks because of a groin problem.

Curd; Golden, O'Connell, Bush, Bainbridge; Booty, White; Cashman, Brunt, Allarakhia; Rush

Whitworth; Weir, Carragher, Burns; Barrett, Power, Gray, Miller; Burrowes, Walsh; Saydee

Running away with the National League crown, Boreham Wood will fear no foe at the moment - not even the 2013 FA Cup champions.

Wigan are struggling in League One and have faltered on the road so far this term, with another away loss likely at Meadow Park.

#2265. He played with Scott Parker at Charlton Athletic - Then he was jailed for £15m fraud

Centre-back Richard Rufus was in the same Charlton Athletic team as Scott Parker, but despite an eleven-year career at The Valley, his post-retirement life has been one misstep after another.

The life of the professional footballer can be an extremely insecure one. All players are one serious injury from their career being over, while a downturn in form could mean that their next contract could be their last.

A lucky few make it through to their mid-to-late thirties without any mishaps, but there's also a number who don't even make it that far, and in the case of the former Charlton Athletic defender Richard Rufus, a series of injuries resulted in his career ending at just 29 years of age.

But Rufus's post-playing career has not been a good one. The former centre-back has repeatedly found himself in trouble with the law, to an extent that, despite having been inducted into Charlton's hall of fame in 2013, he's at risk of becoming better known for this post-career proclivities than he was for his talents on the pitch.

As a player, Richard Rufus was the definition of a one-club man. Born in Lewisham in 1975, he joined Charlton Athletic as a youth and progressed through the club's academy, making his debut for them as a 20-year-old against Wolverhampton Wanderers in April 1995.

He would go on to spend 11 years at The Valley, and the early years of his career suggested that he could go on to get to the very top of the game. He was selected six times for the England under-21s over the course of 1996 and 1997, and by this time he was an established face at the heart of the Charlton central defence.

In 1998, he was part of the Charlton team which returned to the top-flight for the first time since 1990, though his Premier League debut against Newcastle United in August 1998 only lasted 25 minutes before he was sent off, and Charlton were relegated back to what was then still known as the First Division again.

On this occasion, though, Charlton bounced straight back up at the end of the 1999-2000 season, winning the First Division title, with Rufus scoring six goals in 44 appearances for them. His team-mate at the centre of the Charlton defence for a while in the early 2000s, Gary Rowett, later stated that Rufus was "on the radar" for a full England call-up by this time.

Among the other players in the Charlton team of that time was Scott Parker, who would go on to hit considerable heights with West Ham United, Spurs and Fulham, and who's since made a successful career for himself as a manager.

Charlton Athletic made significant profit on Darren Bent and Scott Parker.

But while the new century started explosively well for Rufus and his club, it didn't take long for injury problems to start mounting up. Rufus made 32 appearances for Charlton in the 2001-02 season, but the following season he was limited to just ten as the result of a knee injury.

The start of the 2003-04 season suggested that he had recovered from his knee problems, but behind the scenes the injury returned, and throughout the year he underwent a series of operations. It looked briefly as though he was back when he appeared on their bench for a League Cup match against Luton Town in September 2003 which Charlton won on penalty kicks.

But in truth, the injury hadn't fully cleared, and after one final operation carried out by the renowned orthopedic surgeon Richard Steadman in the USA failed, he was forced to retire from the professional game at the age of just 29 in the summer of 2004. In total, he made 254 appearances for Charlton over 11 years, scoring twelve goals. In 2013, he was inducted into the club's hall of fame.

Rufus's post-playing life has been blighted by a succession of run-ins with the law. The problems started in October 2013 when, having set himself up as a financial advisor, he was declared bankrupt with debts totalling £6 million. Among his creditors were the Pentecostal church of which he was a member, which lost £5m, the equivalent to half of a £10.1 million windfall they'd received from the sale of land in Hackney, as well as family members. Rufus had promised his church a 72% return on their investment, but they only managed to claw back £1.4 million from it.

From here on, things only seemed to spiral. By May 2015, his bankruptcy had been discharged, but he was in court again following an investigation into misappropriation of funds and the unregulated taking of deposits. It was established that Rufus had operated a £16m Ponzi scheme involving more than 100 investors, including members of his family and congregation members of churches he had attended, pocketing more than £3 million. He received a 15-year bankruptcy restriction order for his troubles.

The matter ended up being treated as a criminal matter, and in 2019 he was charged with fraud, money laundering and carrying out a regulated activity without authorisation. It took a long time for the case to come to trial - it wasn't heard until November 2022 - but in January 2023 the former player was convicted and sentenced to seven-and-a-half years in prison. It was heard that Rufus had used his former career as a professional footballer to project a status of wealth while living off the money that he had promised to invest. In total, he paid back £7.4 million of investors’ money and lost them £8.7 million.

Both Gary Rowett and Scott Parker have gone on to have successful careers in management. Rowett has managed at eight different EFL clubs, while Parker has won promotion from the Championship three times and has recently landed a plum job at West Ham United. In stark contrast to that, though, the sort of management that Richard Rufus went into once his playing career ended up being of a very different sort, and he's lost pretty much everything as a result.

#2264. Will Lisandro Martinez play against Leeds after explosive rant?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Lisandro Martinez could return to Manchester United’s starting line-up against Leeds United this weekend, with the defender reportedly available for selection after the controversy surrounding his comments about the club’s summer transfer business.

TEAMtalk transfer insider Graeme Bailey has suggested Michael Carrick could bring the Argentine straight back into the team for the trip to Elland Road. The fixture is expected to be a hostile occasion, making Martinez’s potential involvement an interesting one.

“I am told that Martinez is available for selection this weekend, and there is every chance Michael Carrick starts him,” Bailey said.

The defender recently found himself in trouble after questioning whether Manchester United had done enough in the summer transfer window. He apologised to the club’s hierarchy and was fined, but the incident has not apparently created a divide between him and the manager.

Bailey also dismissed suggestions that Martinez’s comments were an attack on Michael Carrick, insisting the defender and his teammates continue to support the head coach.

“Martinez was not critical of Carrick – far from it. This isn’t a repeat of something like Mo Salah criticising Arne Slot last season. It is almost the opposite.”

According to Bailey, the United dressing room remains firmly behind Carrick despite the team’s disappointing start to the campaign. Senior players reportedly believe the current manager is still the right person to lead the team, rather than bringing in another face to replace him.

“Martinez and the squad are 100% behind Carrick. They know there is pressure after a poor start to the season, but they do not want to see a change.”

Bailey went on to suggest that the fallout may have strengthened the bond between the players and their manager.

“Martinez and the senior players firmly believe nobody can come in and do a better job than Carrick. If anything, this has brought the group closer together.”
